#5000. Morning Minute: CFTC Clears Stock Perps, Gets Sued the Same Day

Morning Minute: CFTC Clears Stock Perps, Gets Sued the Same Day

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

⚖️ CFTC Clears Stock Perps, Gets Sued the Same Day

A perpetual future is a leveraged bet on a price that never expires. You hold it as long as you can meet margin and keep paying a funding rate. It’s analog product would be options in TradFi, with a few differences (primarily no expiration date). Crypto traders have used them for years, mostly on offshore venues like Binance and onchain platforms like Hyperliquid, because U.S. law had no clear home for the product.

On Tuesday the CFTC said Americans can now trade them on individual stocks.

Hours later, Wall Street sued to stop it.

The approval came as a statement from CFTC Chairman Michael Selig. Equity perpetual futures are now tradable on CFTC-registered exchanges, classified as security futures products under the Commodity Futures Modernization Act of 2000, which puts them under joint CFTC and SEC oversight.

The list of venues that can actually do this is short, and it was assembled quietly. A firm needs to be a CFTC-designated contract market and separately notice-registered with the SEC as a national securities exchange for security futures products. Seven entities hold both today: Cboe, CME, Coinbase, Kalshi, Bitnomial, Nadex, and ICE Futures U.S. Five of those seven filed inside a single 30-day window in September.

Kalshi is furthest along, with a filing covering 56 to 58 names and a launch expected within days. Coinbase has filed to list perps on Apple, Tesla and Nvidia. So those two are immediate winners. But Hyperliquid and Lighter, the top crypto perps protocols, are next in line for potential CFTC approvals and are likely future winners as well (LIT +10% on the day).

Then came the lawsuit. A new group called the Capital Markets Alliance filed in the Southern District of New York, naming Cboe, Citadel Securities and the Healthy Markets Association among its members. Its argument runs on two tracks.

The group wants the policy eliminated. The American Securities Association piled on separately, pointing out that Congress banned selling security-based swaps to retail investors at all.

A CFTC spokesman called the suit “troubling,” adding that “our markets should be governed by law, not litigation campaigns intended to block innovation.” The CME already tried this once, suing over the CFTC’s crypto perps approval on the theory that perpetuals are swaps rather than futures. That one went nowhere.

Well, remember that old phrase: “First they ignore you, then they laugh at you, then they fight you, then you win."

We’re at the fight stage. Next stop, winning.

#4996. Justin Sun seeks $264K sanctions over World Liberty’s UAE disclosure

Sun plans to refile his token-freeze lawsuit in California state court after the sanctions motion is resolved. A hearing is set for Dec. 10.

Tron founder Justin Sun is seeking $263,821.20 in sanctions against World Liberty Financial and its lawyers, accusing the Trump family-linked crypto company of submitting a misleading court disclosure about its ownership in a legal dispute over his $45 million WLFI token investment.

In a federal court filing, Sun and two co-plaintiffs alleged that World Liberty initially listed Florida as its sole citizenship before amending its disclosure nearly three months later to identify 22 individuals and entities in its ownership chain, including those in the United Arab Emirates.

“World Liberty presumably always knew who its owners were,” Sun said. “When we asked how this happened, World Liberty refused to tell us.”

The amended disclosure came six days after a federal judge questioned World Liberty’s bid to move Sun’s claims into arbitration. World Liberty’s lawyers subsequently argued that the company’s UAE citizenship deprived the federal court of jurisdiction because foreign parties were on both sides of the lawsuit.

World Liberty Financial did not immediately respond to Cointelegraph’s request for comment.

Related: Circle shareholder sues Trump, World Liberty and Steve Witkoff over crypto deals

In April, Sun sued World Liberty, alleging fraud and the unlawful freezing of his tokens. World Liberty filed a defamation lawsuit against Sun in May, alleging false statements and violations of token-sale terms. Sun denied the allegations.

The legal back-and-forth followed a January 2025 agreement by an Abu Dhabi-backed investment vehicle to purchase a 49% stake in World Liberty for $500 million.

Sun previously backed the Trump family’s crypto ventures by investing in World Liberty and buying President Donald Trump’s TRUMP memecoin. He topped the token’s leaderboard for a May 2025 dinner with Trump, with holdings valued at about $18.5 million.

In an Aug. 26 email included in the court exhibits, World Liberty’s lawyer Sam Cleveland said his team had “recently” learned of additional citizenship information. He maintained that World Liberty already had UAE citizenship when Sun filed the lawsuit in April.

Sun’s lawyers subsequently sought corporate records and testimony about World Liberty’s ownership and when its attorneys learned of the UAE connection. They allege that World Liberty provided limited documentation but declined to explain why its original disclosure omitted that information.

According to attorney Edward Moss’s sworn declaration, the $263,821.20 request covers fees for four attorneys’ work opposing arbitration. It excludes other lawyers’ fees and costs related to jurisdictional discovery and the sanctions motion.

Sun said Tuesday he plans to refile in California state court after the sanctions motion is resolved. A hearing is scheduled for Dec. 10.

#4994. Even As a Wicker Man, Alexander Skarsgård’s Got Game. This Scent Will Give You Some Too.

The actor is the face of Valentino’s newest fragrance, Vendetta.

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

IF ANYONE CAN MAKE RATTAN hot, it’s Alexander Skarsgård. The actor’s latest film project, about a craftsman who creates a wicker husband (Skarsgård) for a lonely woman (played by Olivia Colman), has been an unlikely hit since its release in theaters last week. But basket weave isn’t the only material Skarsgård’s rocking—he’s been killing it on the red carpet lately, too.

There was the gold Saint Laurent suit earlier this month at the BFI London Film Festival, the sick Chopova Lowena skirt to promote Wicker at the Los Angeles premiere last week, and the head-to-toe Valentino outfit he wore at the house’s show during Paris Fashion Week. It seems there’s nothing Alexander Skarsgård can’t infuse with his unique brand of Swedish swag.

Shop the new Valentino Vendetta fragrance.

So, it should come as no surprise when Valentino tapped the 50-year-old star as one of the faces of its brand-new fragrance pillar, Vendetta (alongside Dakota Johnson for the women’s version). Unlike Valentino’s most famous scent family, Born in Roma, which features a spiked pyramid design, Vendetta’s bottle pays homage to the brand’s classic V motif, with a rich, sensual juice created by master perfumer Marie Salamagne.

You’ll get the sensuality from the campaign imagery, photographed by iconic duo Inez & Vinoodh, and the epic accompanying video, directed by French filmmaker Romain Gavras, but what about the fragrance itself?

“It has a boldness and a kind of self-assertive quality to it,” Skarsgård tells me when I ask him to describe the scent. “It’s not quite as heavy as I thought it would be. That first note of ginger has a lightness to it and then it evolves into those richer, deeper notes that last longer.”

For his part, Skarsgård says he’s a signature scent kind of guy—a far cry from the scentmaxxing approach of today’s TikTokers, who douse themselves in enough cologne to create their own noxious atmosphere.

“I’m a proponent of finding your fragrance and sticking with it,” he says. “You become associated with that smell, it becomes part of who you are.”

That’s not to say he hasn’t had his share of missteps. Born in Stockholm, Skarsgård began acting at a young age—his first film role came when he was just seven—but quit as a teenager to join the Swedish navy and, eventually, attend university. Along the way, he learned some valuable fragrance lessons, as most guys do. “I think when I was a teenager, I overdid it a lot,” he says. “You want to marry your scents, so it’s a combination of the fragrance and your own natural scent.”

A woody-spicy addition to the Valentino fragrance family, Vendetta is the first new scent pillar from the brand since its blockbuster Born in Roma.

It’s quite an evolution for a lad who equates childhood with the unmistakable aroma of sausage. When I ask Skarsgård about his first connection to fragrance—that unmistakably transformative power of scent—it’s the sausage factory that faced his front door.

“To this day, it really brings me back, and they’re fond memories,” he says. “Whenever I smell sausage, I’m an eight-year-old again. I’m getting ready for school, seeing my buddies, thinking about this girl I had a crush on. It strikes a chord.”

Of course, processed meat isn’t a key part of the olfactive experience of Vendetta Uomo. It opens with a sharp note of sparkling ginger with the typical brightness of a top note. From there, a warm, rich cinnamon liquor accord envelops the nose and gives way to earthy, grounding patchouli and woods. It’s at turns inviting and accessible while still drawing you in with something seductive and unique.

It’s sex in a spritz, the idea being that you too can have the appeal of a 6'4" Norse god. Ironically, Skarsgård’s everyday life couldn’t be more different from what he says most people see when he’s walking the red carpet or strutting his stuff on screen.

“When I’m not working, I’m trying to spend as little time as possible thinking about [my skincare and sartorial choices],” he says. “It’s all about comfort. I’ve been on a shoot for four months and I’ve been wearing the same shorts and flip-flops for months.”

But isn’t that part of the appeal of a fragrance—the ability to be a different person, someone out of your ordinary? Sure, you could be wearing a pair of sweats and a hat, but with a spray of Vendetta, you’re suddenly wearing a leopard-print overcoat on top of it.

It’s a good look. Just ask Alexander Skarsgård.

Shop the new Valentino Vendetta fragrance.

#4992. Crédit Mutuel Arkéa Confirms Cyberattack After ZachXBT Ties It to $133M Crypto Theft

Crédit Mutuel Arkéa Confirms Cyberattack After ZachXBT Ties It to $133M Crypto Theft

French banking group Crédit Mutuel Arkéa has confirmed that it was hit by a cyberattack that allowed fraudulent operations, a day after onchain investigator ZachXBT named it as the alleged victim of a crypto theft of more than $133 million.

In a statement shared with Decrypt on Wednesday, the bank said the incident was "closed and now under control," and that operations were resuming gradually after it put stronger security measures in place. It said customers' assets were not affected, and that the attack had only delayed the execution of some transfers.

The bank did not mention crypto or say how much was taken. The amounts affected are still being assessed, but are "in any event not of a nature to call into question the solidity and financial health of the group," it said, according to Decrypt's translation.

Crédit Mutuel Arkéa said it informed the relevant supervisory authorities as soon as it detected the attack, and temporarily suspended some transactions as a precaution to prevent further fraudulent movements. It also warned customers to watch out for phishing attempts following the attack. Decrypt has reached out to Crédit Mutuel Arkéa for further comment, including on whether the losses involved crypto.

"Disclosing the EU financial institution exploited for $133M+ is allegedly Credit Mutuel Arkea, a French bank with $235B in assets," ZachXBT wrote on his Telegram channel on Tuesday. "The attack on the bank occurred for multiple days. I suspect the amount stolen is larger when accounting for OTC activity."

In an update on Tuesday, he said he had identified a further $27 million in exchange withdrawals by the attacker, bringing the total to more than $133 million, and that some of those withdrawals took place after his initial disclosure. He also published four Ethereum addresses he said were linked to the theft.

"My working theory is a large financial institution in Western Europe was exploited and it's currently being covered up," ZachXBT wrote at the time, accusing it of not having conducted "proper incident disclosure" and thereby preventing law enforcement from freezing some $30 million in stolen funds.

He did not say how he had identified the bank, or how the attacker gained access to its exchange accounts.

The mutual banking group has been preparing to move into crypto. In May, French financial newspaper L'Agefi reported that it had applied for a license under MiCA, the EU's crypto rulebook, to offer crypto services to its retail customers and to provide them as a white-label service through its Arkéa Banking Services division.

The allegation comes days after the $6.3 billion hack of Binance, the largest crypto theft on record, which the FBI has attributed to North Korean hackers. ZachXBT said the attacker in the suspected EU theft remains unidentified, but that he could say "with a high degree of confidence it is NOT Lazarus Group," the North Korean hacking collective, "based on multiple laundering indicators."

This story has been updated to add comment from Crédit Mutuel Arkéa.

#4990. Binance hack may be ‘temporary setback’ for crypto, Grayscale’s Pandl says

Zach Pandl highlighted institutional custody safeguards as crypto markets recovered some losses following the record $6.3 billion Binance hack.

Binance’s $6.3 billion hack could temporarily hurt confidence in crypto markets but would ultimately lead to stronger security practices across exchanges, according to Grayscale.

“It also underscores the importance of institutional custody, such as offered by US-based crypto [exchange-traded products],” said Grayscale’s head of research, Zach Pandl, in a Tuesday report.

Binance suffered a record $6.3 billion hack Thursday and pledged to cover all customer losses. The FBI attributed the attack to North Korea. Kim Yo Jong, sister of North Korean leader Kim Jong Un, dismissed the allegations as “absurd rhetoric.”

The hack was followed by a market sell-off, with Bitcoin falling below $77,000 and BNB losing more than 8% over 24 hours by early Friday.

By Wednesday, total crypto market capitalization had rebounded 2% over the past 24 hours to about $2.63 trillion, after touching a weekly low of about $2.55 trillion earlier in the day. The recovery left the market about $100 billion below its Oct. 28 level.

Bitwise’s head of research, André Dragosch, told Cointelegraph that recent outflows from US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds were “related to a short-term risk-off environment rather than a structural change in institutional sentiment.”

#4988. Do Your Muscles Need a ‘Creatine Washout?’ It Depends.

The benefits might be more mental than physical.

CREATINE IS THE prom king of social media—everyone’s talking about it. And not always nice things, either. Because for all the (very warranted) complements about how creatine can help you grow stronger, there’s also some (baseless) behind-the-back whispers about how it can make your hair fall out, poison you with heavy metals, and yes even kill you.

Between this polarization, there’s the seemingly reasonable personal stance of Andrew Huberman, PhD: Take creatine, but also take a creatine break.

Just in case you’re unfamiliar, a Huberman is a neuroscientist and associate professor in the Departments of Neurobiology and Ophthalmology at Stanford University School of Medicine. And he only hosts one of the most popular health podcasts of all time, Huberman Lab. When Huberman says anything about anything, people listen.

So when Huberman posted that he does a “creatine washout” every four months “just to see what effect it continues to have over time,” you have to wonder.

IT’S THE TIME period after you stop supplementing with creatine. The term is used frequently in scientific research that assesses the effects of creatine supplementation, often in athletic performance studies.

But, admittedly, there aren’t that many studies that look at the creatine washout phase itself. The most recent goes all the way back to 2004: “Effects of repeated creatine supplementation on muscle, plasma, and urine creative levels.”

In that study researchers found that levels of muscle phosphocreatine—that’s the amount of creatine stored within muscle cells—did not return to pre-supplementation baselines even after a 30-day washout period.

Since that 2004 study, there hasn’t been any supportive—or contradictory—science, but the lead author says the findings hold up more than 20 years later.

“Four to six weeks is a typical response and depends on dietary creatine, response to supplement, etc.,” says Eric Rawson, PhD, FACSM, Chair of the Department of Health, Nutrition, and Exercise Science at Messiah University, and a co-author of the 2004 washout study.

MAYBE, BUT NOT for reasons you might think.

“Questions about creatine washout and/or cycling supplementation are best handled from both physiological and behavioral perspectives,” says Rawson. “When I am asked about washout and cycling, I always ask people if they have similar concerns about their multivitamin. Or their calcium supplements. Or whey protein.”

Meaning, do you have the same washout concerns about creatine as you do your other supplements? Probably not.

“There appears to be a lingering concern about long-term creatine supplementation that is not supported by data.”

That said, Rawson says that some people, including many athletes, can feel overwhelmed by demands of maintaining their fitness edge—to the point where it’s good to take a break for mental health.

“Taking an off-season break from some of the usual routines, which includes, training, meal frequency, macro-nutrient intake goals, and the daily dietary supplement routine, can provide a break and perhaps some relief from the usual routine,” he says.

How long a break is sort of up to you and your long-term goals. But let your body and brain be the judge of that—not whatever you hear on social.

Paul Kita is a Deputy Editor at Men's Health, where he has covered food, cooking, nutrition, supplements, grooming, tech, travel, and fatherhood at the brand for more than 15 years. He is also the author of two Men's Health cookbooks, Guy Gourmet and A Man, A Pan, A Plan, and the winner of a James Beard Award.

#4986. Wall Street’s Plumbing Goes Onchain as Morgan Stanley Tokenizes Securities

Wall Street’s Plumbing Goes Onchain as Morgan Stanley Tokenizes Securities

Morgan Stanley has started moving U.S. stocks and Treasuries onto blockchain rails, using Wall Street’s own plumbing to do it.

The bank said Tuesday that it’s among the first to tokenize real-world assets through the DTCC Tokenization Service, converting U.S. equities and Treasuries into tokenized form.

The service, which went live Oct. 28, is run by the Depository Trust Co., a subsidiary of the Depository Trust & Clearing Corp. The DTC is the post-trade backbone of U.S. markets, with more than $114 trillion in assets under custody.

“The hands-on experience of integrating tokenized securities with established market infrastructure will inform the continued development of the firm’s digital asset capabilities as the market evolves,” said Amy Oldenburg, Morgan Stanley’s head of digital assets strategy.

The service lets eligible firms convert securities held at DTC into tokenized “digital twins” and back again, delivering them to digital wallets of their choice. The tokens keep the same ownership rights, investor protections and economic entitlements as the originals, according to DTCC, and are designed to let firms move collateral and tap new pools of liquidity within minutes. The service currently runs on the Canton Network and DTCC’s private Besu network, with more blockchains planned for 2027.

Morgan Stanley is one of 20 DTC participants that have signed up so far, alongside Bank of America, Citi, J.P. Morgan, Fidelity Investments, Citadel Securities, and UBS, among others. DTCC said it developed the service with input from more than 150 firms.

The launch caps a regulatory path that began in December 2025, when the SEC gave DTC a no-action letter allowing it to tokenize Russell 1000 stocks, major index ETFs and U.S. Treasuries. DTCC has also worked with Chainlink on tokenized collateral management and laid out its plans for the service earlier this year as major Wall Street firms signed on.

The bank-led approach contrasts with crypto-native efforts to tokenize stocks. Fifty-six companies, including Tesla, SpaceX, Amazon, and Nvidia, have objected to having their shares traded on OKXICE TSV, a planned tokenized stock venue from OKX and NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange. Under the SEC’s innovation exemption, those objections bar the venue from listing third-party tokenized versions of their stock.

Demand is building, but the market is still young. The International Monetary Fund found last month that tokenized stocks trade mostly outside U.S. market hours, though they remain more volatile and less liquid than their traditional counterparts.

#4983. I’m a Doctor and I Won’t Touch Honey Packs. Here’s What I Do Before Sex Instead

Ahead, three tried-and-true strategies for better, longer-lasting erections.

IF YOU HAVE difficulty getting erections or simply want to spruce up your sex life, chances are you’ve heard about honey packs. The sachets of the sweet stuff are all over social media, promising enhanced sexual performance through stronger, long-lasting erections.

At first glance, you might find this a “natural” alternative for young guys who don’t want pills or painful-sounding ED procedures like shock wave therapy. After all, the packets do feature pictures of honey and bees swarming on honeycombs, and honey is usually one of the first ingredients—a food many dudes already drizzle in oatmeal and tea. What could go wrong?

MH did a deep dive last year into whether honey packs actually work. The TL;DR version is that despite the alluring promise of near-instant, rock-hard erections, you’re really taking a gamble on what you’re putting in your body. Natural ingredients like honey and ginseng provide a “trivial” performance-enhancing effect and multiple honey pack supplement companies have been flagged by the FDA for having hidden and unknown dosages of ingredients not included in the label, like tadalafil (the active ingredient in Cialis), sildenafil (the active ingredient in Viagra), and acetaminophen.

That’s why doctors have big safety concerns about honey packs, says Scott Miller, MD, a urologist and director of robotic surgery at Wellstar Health System. “If they can’t tell me what’s in them, then it’s an absolute no.” So if honey packs are out, what are some ways to help you get hard and stay hard? We turned to the experts, asking urologists what they do to prepare in lieu of popping a pack.

HONEY PACKS (AND even gas station dick pills) aren’t worth your time or money. But not all supplements need to be ruled out, says Philip Werthman, MD, a urologist and director of the Center for Male Reproductive Medicine and Vasectomy Reversal. For guys with mild ED or who just want to optimize their libido, he recommends L-arginine supplements.

L-arginine is an amino acid that supports blood flow through nitric oxide production. Nitric oxide allows blood vessels to open fully and allow more blood flow to come into the penis.

“The penis is a hemodynamic organ. It works on blood flow. There has to be stimulation and a relationship that is functional between the nerves and the blood vessels,” says Dr. Werthman. “Anything that helps nitric oxide stick around longer will cause more blood flow to come in and maybe give a better quality erection.”

If possible, he recommends choosing L-arginine supplements containing L-citrulline (a precursor to L-arginine) or pycnogenol. The latter is an antioxidant made from the bark of a tree and is a powerful antioxidant used often in the fertility world, says Dr. Werthman. In concert with L-arginine, the combo helps to boost erections.

There is one caveat. For the effects to really work, you need to take this supplement on a daily basis. A single scoop the night before a sexual encounter won’t make much of a difference.

“It’s the kind of thing you put into your supplement stack,” says Dr. Werthman. Beyond healthy erections, nitric oxide helps with increasing blood flow all over the body, potentially providing extra benefits to your brain, heart, and muscles if you’re working out.

Buyer Beware: Supplements are not FDA-regulated, and some can get sketchy. Consider getting a product tested and certified by a third-party testing facility to verify the supplement’s safety and quality.

OYSTERS AND CHAMPAGNE get a lot of hype as aphrodisiacs. But Dr. Werthman says the true value is eating foods high in L-arginine. Here are some examples:

Additionally, watermelon is one of the best foods to eat on the day you’re having sex because of its high citrulline content, says Dr. Werthman. So, while spontaneous sex is great, scheduling sex can help you fuel your body (and boner) accordingly.

As for what’s on the don’t eat list? Nothing, but both urologists say it’s best to avoid alcohol before sex. You might gain a little liquid courage, but the buzz kills erections. Alcohol slows down brain signals related to arousal and decreases the ability of blood vessels to trap blood in the penis.

CORTISOL, ALSO KNOWN as your body’s stress hormone, is one of the biggest killers of testosterone and erections. When your body is in fight-or-flight mode, Dr. Werthman says, the last thing it’s thinking about is having sex.

The trademarks of a stressful lifestyle—lack of sleep, long working hours, and a poor diet—can all create barriers to intimacy, says Dr. Miller. Setting aside time to take care of yourself physically will help with performing at your best.

For example, a meditation session or some light stretching can relieve some stress temporarily. You also might want to consider a quick workout. Now, Dr. Werthman warns that a single sweat session won’t do much for your penile health (over time, consistent exercise does support blood flow and cardiovascular health, helping you last longer). But in terms of mental health, the stress-relieving benefits of pushups or a quick jog can help you get amped for the main event. Plus, the endorphins in your body can shake off any pre-sex nerves and make you feel more confident in your body. One study involving over 128,000 people found all types of physical activity—walking, resistance training, Pilates, and yoga—effective in improving anxiety symptoms.

Granted, for some people, sex itself is the stress reliever. But if you tense up thinking about the actual act, Dr. Werthman advises taking it slow. Get out of your head and focus on increasing erotic arousal through kissing and other forms of foreplay, then seeing if you want to go further. “It’s about being comfortable with your partner,” he says. “You want a dynamic where if you get a fail-to-launch situation, they understand. Because it happens—not every encounter leads to a touchdown.”

#4982. Canton’s CC rises 4% as SEC staff view it as ‘digital commodity’

SEC staff’s no-action letter covers CC earned by running infrastructure and applications, but is not a binding ruling on its legal status.

Canton Network’s native cryptocurrency rose 4% on Wednesday after the US Securities and Exchange Commission’s (SEC) Division of Corporation Finance issued a no-action letter stating it views the token as a “digital commodity.”

Canton Coin (CC) traded at $0.098 at 10:27 am UTC, but was still down roughly 4.2% over the past week, according to CoinMarketCap.

In its Tuesday letter, SEC staff accepted the explanation that CC’s value comes from how the network operates and demand for the token, rather than a central team’s efforts to generate profits for holders.

The division also said it would not recommend enforcement action over CC’s issuance without securities registration, provided the tokens are created under the conditions described by Canton.

In Friday’s letter to the SEC, Canton developer Digital Asset said network participants earn CC by operating infrastructure and applications, with the protocol determining how many tokens they can mint based on their activity.

The SEC issued guidance in March distinguishing digital commodities, such as Bitcoin and Ether, from securities. It said these tokens are not themselves securities, although the way they are offered and sold can still be subject to securities laws.

The SEC’s response reflects its staff’s position and is not a binding ruling on CC’s legal status.

SEC staff previously issued token-related no-action letters to crypto projects, including DoubleZero (2Z) in September 2025 and Solana-native decentralized physical infrastructure network (DePIN) project Fuse in November 2025.

#4981. Bitcoin Rebounds After Democratic Midterm Sweep, But Crypto Faces Tougher Fight in Washington

Bitcoin Rebounds After Democratic Midterm Sweep, But Crypto Faces Tougher Fight in Washington

Bitcoin and Ethereum recovered Wednesday after Democrats won control of both houses of Congress in the U.S. midterm elections, despite the results likely making the crypto industry's push for friendlier federal rules more difficult.

The crypto market recovery, in technical terms, appears to be more a natural bounce, and in line with expectations, instead of a trend reversal or a FOMO-driven spike. The overnight selloff also reversed as U.S. stock futures bounced back, making it hard to pin the recovery on politics alone.

Bitcoin briefly hit a six-week low before a bounce that took it back to just above $80,000 once again, above its average price of the last 50 days, which is a solid bullish sign after weeks of bearish correction.

Ethereum, meanwhile, spiked 7% to $2,434, while U.S. stocks on the whole jumped around 1.6%, with the Dow Jones Industrial Average leading the way with a 2.5% rally.

Analysts who spoke to Decrypt said the Democratic sweep had largely been priced into crypto markets before election night. A rebound in Bitcoin therefore says less about investors welcoming the election result than about how much bad news they were already expecting.

The industry has spent more than $300 million supporting crypto-friendly candidates from both parties across the 2024 and 2026 election cycles. About $176 million went toward more than 100 races this year alone.

That spending was aimed partly at getting legislation through Congress that would give digital asset businesses clearer rules. The Clarity Act, a major piece of that effort, would define how federal regulators oversee different types of crypto assets and trading activity.

The bill was in trouble before Tuesday's election. Senators voted 49-50 against advancing it in September, falling short of the 60 votes needed to move past a procedural hurdle. That was not a final vote on the legislation, but it stopped the measure from reaching a full debate.

Negotiations had already run into disputes over restrictions on government officials' crypto business interests and the treatment of rewards paid on stablecoins, tokens designed to hold a fixed value. President Donald Trump's personal and family crypto ventures became a particular point of conflict for Democratic lawmakers.

Democratic control of Congress makes a quick return to the same legislative deal less likely, but it does not automatically undo what the industry secured under Trump. His administration has eased enforcement, established a strategic Bitcoin reserve and overseen the passage of a federal stablecoin law.

The distinction matters more for exchanges, token issuers, and other firms seeking clear legal permissions than for Bitcoin's day-to-day price. Analysts told Decrypt that liquidity, interest rates and investment flows still exert a stronger influence on the cryptocurrency itself.

The broader crypto market has shed roughly $300 billion over the past month, bringing its total value to about $2.67 trillion. Rising borrowing costs and worries about returns on artificial intelligence investments have pushed traders toward less risky assets like bonds or gold.

Major Bitcoin exchange-traded funds, which offer exposure to the asset through traditional brokerage accounts, recorded $215 million in net withdrawals in October. That brought their year-to-date net outflows to $1.84 billion.

The new Congress takes office in January 2027. Unless lawmakers revive the Clarity Act before the current session ends, the industry will need to start a new legislative effort with Democrats controlling both chambers.

#4976. Tech, AI Stocks Split After Democratic Sweep: Microsoft Jumps, Tesla and Nvidia Tank

Tech, AI Stocks Split After Democratic Sweep: Microsoft Jumps, Tesla and Nvidia Tank

Technology stocks delivered a mixed performance Wednesday after Democrats swept the U.S. midterm elections, with investors weighing the prospect of tougher artificial intelligence regulation against expectations that a divided government could limit major policy changes.

The Nasdaq-100 climbed 1.14% to 28,500, recovering from an overnight selloff that briefly pushed index futures down more than 2%.

But beneath the broader market rebound, individual technology stocks told sharply different stories.

Microsoft jumped 6.01% to $552.32, while Google parent Alphabet rose 4.63% to $292.99, according to intraday charts reviewed Wednesday.

While software stocks jumped, hardware stocks corrected to the downside. AI chipmaking giant Nvidia fell 2.19% to $202.17, Tesla dropped 3.61% to $384.10, and Intel lost 5.80% to $57.04.

That said, none has yet shown a heavy bearish trend. Even Nvidia, which is going through the most obvious price correction, remains bullish on the longer trend as the prices test a weak support near the $200 price zone.

Bitcoin also recovered strongly, trading around $80,512, up 4.12% in the day, after an initial election-related selloff.

The conflicting moves reflect the uncertainty over what Democratic control of Congress will mean for technology companies that have benefited from the Donald Trump administration's approach to deregulation.

According to Reuters, investors are particularly concerned about greater scrutiny of AI data centers—the hardware camps of the AI industry—following Democratic victories in states such as Texas and Georgia, where large infrastructure projects have become politically contentious.

The AI sector was already facing questions over enormous infrastructure spending and whether those investments would generate adequate returns.

One of Wednesday's biggest losers was Trump Media & Technology Group, the company behind Truth Social.

Shares plunged 14.29% to $5.10, extending a decline that had been underway long before Democrats secured control of Congress.

The stock had already lost approximately 55% of its value year-to-date before Tuesday's vote.

Technical indicators show traders had been bearish on the stock for months. Trump Media has been in a technical pattern that traders refer to as a “death cross” since March 2025, and has lost nearly 90% of its market cap since its 2024 cap. A death cross happens when the average price of the last 200 days becomes greater than that of the last 50 days, suggesting the asset is losing value way too quickly.

The Nasdaq-100's recovery is encouraging for technology bulls, but the index remains well below its October highs above 31,000. The 28,000 area represents an immediate support zone, while a move back above 29,000 would strengthen the short-term recovery.

Bitcoin's recovery above $80,000 offers another indication that risk appetite has not disappeared entirely.

Still, Wednesday's split between technology winners and losers suggests investors are becoming increasingly selective about their exposure to the AI boom—and the political risks surrounding it.

#4973. The Men's Health Ultimate Guide to Peptides

A science-backed guide to understanding the use, side effects, and supporting research behind the emerging substances.

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

AS THE CURIOSITY around peptides grows, the FDA has made efforts in removing a portion of the 19 synthetic peptides from the Category 2 Bulk Substances List. This is kind of a huge deal because when the FDA classifies a substance as Category 2 they believe that it carries significant safety risks, and prohibit compounding pharmacies from making it.

In July 2026, an FDA advisory committee met to evaluate evidence for seven popular synthetic peptides on the Category 2 list and recommend whether the FDA should move them onto the Category 1 list, which allows them to be made. Another five will be under review in 2027.The FDA is not bound to the advisory committee’s recommendations.

The panel voted to loosen the restrictions on BPC-157 and TB-500 (those make up the infamous “Wolverine Stack”), as well as KPV, MOTS-C, Epitalon and Semax. It voted no for Emedeltide; that’s the one people reach for to help their sleep. None of the compounds scored a unanimous vote. This October, US Health Secretary Robert F. Kennedy Jr. pushed forward a policy to temporarily increase access to these six unapproved peptides. The government will not penalize licensed pharmacists in compounding pharmacies for creating and distributing these substances for people with a prescription. The nudge from RFK Jr. to allow the temporary sale of peptides has received mixed reactions.

”The American Peptide Association applauds the efforts of the Trump Administration and HHS to green light certain peptides into production by compound pharmacies,” Jeff Cohen, cofounder of the American Peptide Association wrote in an email to MH. “It is much needed clarity that the peptide industry, clinicians and consumers have hoped and worked for for the past couple years and is a bold step outside the usual pharma driven, FDA managed pathway.”

Motisola Lewis, MD, is an internal medicine physician who runs her own concierge medical practice working to fix a broken medical system. While she focuses on providing personalized care to her patients, she doesn’t agree with expanding access to unapproved peptides before having adequate evidence of their safety and effectiveness. “I want patients to have access to promising treatments, but I also want those treatments to be supported by good science. My concern is that the enthusiasm around peptides has moved much faster than the clinical evidence.” For Dr. Lewis, several questions are still missing that the FDA has not answered: do these peptides actually work and for whom, peptide quality especially from third-party suppliers, and who is in charge of oversight. That doesn’t mean she is unwilling to discuss peptides, which is happening more than ever.

Sritha Rajupet, MD, MPH, chair of the department of family, population, and preventive medicine at Stony Brook Medicine, also has concerns. “Most of these peptides still lack high-quality human data on safety, effectiveness, and appropriate dosing,” Dr. Rajupet says. Meaning that even if the FDA removes the Category 2 label, there’s still not a lot of research around if and how they work—along with any side effects. In the document outlining RFK Jr’s new policy, compounding pharmacists must report “serious adverse events, and product quality issues to FDA and to the States where the products are compounded.”

Still, Dr. Rajupet acknowledges that people are interested in synthetic peptides—and many are already obtaining them through unregulated channels.

Kent Bradley, MD, sees this at his role of chief medical officer at 10X Health, which offers synthetic peptide therapy. “Many of the people reaching for peptides have already exhausted conventional options,” he says. “The issue we are seeing is that individuals may have gotten a peptide without going through legitimate quality checks of a compounded pharmacy and self-administering based on information they are seeing off of social media.”

The world of synthetic peptides is promising, for sure. But there are dangers—some of which are serious (ahem, cancer). Dr. Rajupet says that in order for consumers to protect themselves, they have to study up.

“From a clinical perspective, the priority is balancing harm reduction with scientific rigor—engaging patients in open, nonjudgmental conversations while being clear that many of these therapies remain experimental, with uncertain risks and benefits,” says Dr. Rajupet.

For this reason, we created a one-stop glossary to help answer your questions about the current peptide-palooza we’re in now. Here’s the hype—and actual science.

WELL, KNOW THAT peptides exist naturally. Technically, they’re short chains of amino acids, the building blocks of protein. And not just the kind of protein that builds biceps. Proteins can also act as messengers throughout your body, doing everything from signaling the release a particular hormone to regulating inflammatory responses.

But when we’re talking about the kind of peptides the FDA will consider for deregulation, we’re talking about synthetic peptides delivered through sprays or injection. These drugs work to trigger the same responses as natural peptides, but the science is in its very early stages. Anecdotal evidence from who take peptides have reported improvements in their health, appearance, and muscle performance. But, again, anecdotal.

These are the synthetic peptides that the FDA will consider for deregulation in 2026 and 2027, broken into categories based on their supposed health benefits. And we gave each a thumbs up, thumbs down, or thumbs "meh" to rate just how much the science backs up the supposed benefits.

Known as the fat burning peptide, AOD-9604 mimics human growth hormone to promote lipolysis, or the breakdown of stored fat. Scientists are using the peptide as a possible treatment for obesity and people are taking the peptide recreationally to burn body fat, boost fat metabolism, and support body recomposition. There is also discussion of AOD-9604 helping with joint pain, mobility, and collagen production. One of the supposed benefits of AOD-9604 is that it sheds fat without affecting blood sugar or inducing insulin resistance.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: The studies on the effectiveness and safety of AOD-9604 go back roughly a decade. The research was largely conducted on animals and most of the studies that showed positive results were funded or led by researchers from the pharmaceutical company that developed AOD-9604.

This peptide reportedly protects the cell by providing more “fuel” for increased stamina. So people who report fatigue are taking the peptide to improve energy and metabolism. Because MOTS-C is supposed to target mitochondria, the energy sources of a cell, you’ll see it promoted in longevity stacks.RESEARCH SUMMARY: While there has been extensive research into the physiological function of MOTS-C, there is a lack of clinical trial data testing the safety and effectiveness of the peptide.

Semax is considered a brain-boosting peptide often given as a nasal spray. Anecdotal reports detail improvements in memory, attention, and deeper mental awareness. Semax is supposed to work by raising a protein called brain-derived neurotrophic factor (BDNF) that is important for learning and strengthening connection between brain cells.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: Most of the research in Semax that claims it has neuroprotective effects come from Russia, where the peptide is used in the treatment of people with stroke. The Alzheimer’s Drug Discovery Foundation reviewed the Russian pilot studies in humans and found a lack of strong evidence demonstrating that Semax would improve cognition in healthy individuals or in the prevention of cognitive aging. (Although a recent 2025 study from Russia found evidence of Semax injections reducing amyloid plaques in rat models of Alzheimer’s). Older studies using rats found improved cognition 15 minutes after injection, likely by stimulating BDNF production. There is a lack of safety data, especially in long-term side effects, in humans. One review reported discoloration of the nasal cavity and increased blood sugar levels in people with diabetes. Not great.

Selank is a nootropic touted for reducing anxiety and stress while increasing cognitive performance. Anecdotal evidence calls Selank a “life-changer” for anxiety and sleep, with some people feeling relaxed but not drowsy, as well as more focused.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: Selank’s anxiolytic and cognitive protective effect along with relieving stress have only been studied in rodents and molecular studies. Older mice studies have also found evidence of improved learning and memory performance after a single injection of Selank.

TB-500 is a peptide intended for muscle recovery, increased flexibility, and joint stability. Users often pair the synthetic peptide with BPC-157 to further boost muscle recovery. TB-500 works by binding to a structural protein in cells called actin. By up-regulating actin, TB-500 purportedly speeds cell movement for tissue repair.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: There is a good amount of animal research on Thymosin Beta-4 and its accelerated healing effects on skin and muscle fibers. While there is a body of preclinical research on the peptide, there’s a lack of studies looking at the synthetic peptide TB-500. Plus, there is no clinical evidence in humans that Thymosin Beta-4 or TB-500 repairs muscles or increases athletic performance.

More concerning are studies linking elevated Thymosin Beta-4 levels with the development of cancer. Through the same mechanisms that promote wound healing, Thymosin Beta-4 may enhance tumor angiogenesis, the process where cancer cells stimulate new blood vessels, which provides them oxygen and nutrients to grow and spread.

Known as the “Wolverine” or “Body Protection Compound,” the synthetic peptide reportedly helps with tissue repair and anti-inflammatory effects. Users champion the peptide’s ability to speed recovery from intensive workouts or even injury. BPC-157 does not directly build muscle mass.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: Animal data on BPC-157 showed evidence of the peptide helping with musculoskeletal injuries. However, there is little data showing the effects in humans.

The natural form of this peptide helps with increasing and restoring immune function. It works by enhancing antibody response and T-cell activity. People taking the synthetic, injectable form report immediate improvement in their well-being after long COVID and other immune disorders. Outside of the US, Thymosin Alpha-1 is approved in over 30 countries for treating hepatitis and other infections.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: Five large clinical trials on the peptide have found Thymosin Alpha-1 reduces inflammation and infection in people with severe acute pancreatitis. That’s likely due to the peptide’s ability to rebalance immune cell distribution, including boosting the percentages of CD4+ T cells. Thymosin Alpha-1 has also been shown to enhance vaccine response and help people with severe COVID by repairing damage caused by inflammation. However, one large phase 3 clinical trial in people with sepsis found an injection of thymosin alpha 1 did not improve mortality outcomes.

People taking the synthetic form of this peptide report doing so to reduce inflammation and improve skin health. (Some say it can clear up eczema, psoriasis, and acne.) There are also some people with inflammatory bowel conditions, such as Crohn’s and IBD, who say that KPV has helped their gut health. The peptide reportedly works by inhibiting several pro-inflammatory pathways while preserving other immune responses.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: There’s not sufficient data on KPV to conclusively say it helps in humans. The most cited research on KPV is a 2008 study, which found that the peptide reduced intestinal inflammation in human intestinal cells. Yes, that was many, many years ago.

This neuropeptide reportedly promotes falling asleep faster, as well as increasing how much time you spend in restorative deep, slow-wave sleep. in Stage 3 sleep. Anecdotal evidence of people taking the synthetic form of this peptide states that in helps with staying asleep, increasing sleep duration, and entering deep sleep.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: DSIP research on humans is extremely limited. Most research looking into DSIP’s role in sleep took place in the 1980s and 1990s—with mixed results. While a small clinical trial did report sleep-promoting effects, other studies found it ineffective or from a placebo effect. Research in the 2000s has been focused on animals and how DSIP could help protect against cancer or improve motor ability after stroke.

This peptide has links to sexual function. It works by stimulating the release of the hormone, gonadotropin, which results in an increase of testosterone. People who take synthetic kisspeptin-10 have reported a greater libido, ability to maintain an erection, and enjoy orgasm, along with reduced anxiety—especially surrounding sexual response.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: There is animal and human research that shows kisspeptin-10 stimulates gonadotrophin release. Most notably, a small clinical trial found that men with low sexual desire experienced increases in “penile tumescence” (their term, not ours), as well as greater attraction and arousal. There were no side effects reported.

Epitalon is the natural form of the peptide, and it’s been used to treat chronic insomnia, especially in people who don’t respond to sleep medications. By raising melatonin, the peptide would allegedly regulate circadian rhythms and promote better sleep. Epithalamin is the synthetic peptide and people are reportedly taking it for anti-aging effects, both cognitive and aesthetic.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: Epitalon has not been tested in people for slowing or preventing cognitive decline. Most of the early preclinical and clinical studies on epitalon’s effects on anti-aging, cognition, and sleep come from Russia and mainly from the lab of the now-deceased Dr. Vladimir Kh. Khavinson. The few studies in human cells (note, not actual humans) suggest that epitalon extends telomere length. Telomeres are the protective caps at the end of chromosomes to protect from damage. In terms of safety, there is a lack of data, especially on its short-term and long-term toxicity effects.

The natural GHK-Cu peptide is used for promoting skin health. People using the synthetic drug are using GHK-Cu to stimulate collagen and elastin production to tighten aging skin, reduce wrinkles, fade scars, and repair the skin barrier. People have anecdotally reported thicker, fuller hair after using the peptide as it supposedly reduces inflammation and increases blood circulation to the scalp.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: Topical GHK-Cu is commonly used in cosmetics to increase collagen production, promote wound healing, and improve skin health. It has a good safety profile because it has limited low-level skin absorption. Injectable and oral versions of GHK-Cu may come with several potentially life-threatening risks.

This peptide is a selective agonist of the ghrelin/growth hormone secretagogue receptor (GHSR1) that stimulates the pituitary gland to release growth hormone production. (Yeah, it’s complex.) Just know that people are excited about Ipamorelin because the peptide reportedly increases muscle mass and strength.

Online forums dedicated to Ipamorelin mention that taking the peptide does not increase appetite, cortisol, or prolactin like other growth hormone peptides. In addition to strength improvements, other ancedotes discuss skin and hair benefits. Ipamorelin is typically paired with other peptides, such as CJC-1295.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: Animal studies have shown that Ipamorelin is effective in stimulating the pituitary gland to release growth hormones. However, there is a lack of large clinical trials that show the peptide works in humans.

This peptide binds to receptors in the pituitary gland to stimulate the secretion of growth hormone and production of insulin-like growth factor 1 (IGF-1). People are taking CJC-1295 to build muscle, reduce recovery time, and increase fat metabolism. A common stack among the longevity community is CJC-1295 and Ipamorelin for its synergistic effects to maintain lean muscle, get better sleep, and reduce fatigue.

RESEARCH SUMMARY: While clinical data has shown promising results of CJC-1295 to increase growth hormone production, the peptide is linked to a number of adverse events.

The following five peptides are likely to stay on the Category 2 list because of a lack of clinical evidence and/or significant safety concerns.

SIDE EFFECTS: Infection, potential death, fluctuating blood sugar levels, pancreatitis

SIDE EFFECTS: Increase appetite, possible changes in cortisol levels, and increases in blood sugar levels

USE: “Sunless” tanning and to stimulate erections in men with erectile dysfunction

SIDE EFFECTS: Severe adverse events including melanoma, posterior reversible encephalopathy syndrome, sympathomimetic toxidrome, and priapism

USE: Increasing immune defenses and fight against infections

SIDE EFFECTS: Nonclinical research has found adverse effects on the male reproduction system and potentially cancer development

SIDE EFFECTS: Insufficient information on whether it’s safe for humans

#4971. France backs reporting for self-custodied crypto worth 100K euros or more

The amendment would require taxpayers to report holdings worth at least $112,000, but the measure still needs to survive the budget process.

The French National Assembly approved an amendment that would require taxpayers to report self-custodied crypto holdings totaling at least 100,000 euros ($112,000) to tax authorities.

Amendment I-2701 was adopted on Tuesday in a 150–44 vote, with eight abstentions, according to the Assembly’s official voting record. The threshold would be assessed on Dec. 31 of the relevant tax year.

Separately, the Assembly rejected an amendment that would have exempted gains on crypto payments for goods and services from tax if the payments totaled no more than 1,000 euros ($1,120) during the tax year. The proposal was defeated 69–14, according to the Assembly’s transcript.

The reporting requirement forms part of the 2027 Finance Bill and would need to be retained in the final legislation to become law. The Assembly faces a Nov. 17 deadline to complete its first reading of the bill. Senate debate is scheduled to begin Nov. 20, according to its schedule.

The National Assembly postponed Tuesday’s scheduled vote on the budget’s revenue section for the second time as lawmakers continued debating amendments. The vote was originally scheduled for Oct. 20.

The vote’s postponement itself does not introduce a significant change, but the “likely rejection” of the budget’s revenue section could “result in the rejection of the entire bill in the National Assembly, including provisions adopted by way of amendments,” Faustine Fleuret, global head of public affairs at lending protocol Morpho, told Cointelegraph.

On Friday, the Assembly rejected two proposals that would have applied exit taxes to unrealized crypto gains when taxpayers transferred their tax residence abroad.

Lawmakers also approved an amendment on Oct. 16 that would tax gains from converting crypto into stablecoins. The proposal covers stablecoins classified as electronic money tokens under EU rules and would still need to be retained in the final law.

Related: French Senate adopts bill for automatic sharing of crypto tax data

#4970. What Is x402? How AI Agents Pay With Crypto

What Is x402? How AI Agents Pay With Crypto

Nobody really built the web with payments in mind. Early HTTP specifications included a status code, 402 "Payment Required", that was reserved for a future system of online payments. Then it sat unused for about 30 years. Payments were slow and expensive, and they needed accounts, card details and middlemen who could reverse charges. None of that works for a small payment made by software.

That gap matters more now because AI agents are starting to browse, call APIs and buy services for their users. An agent can't fill in a checkout form or sign up for a monthly plan for every tool it uses. Cloudflare says more than half of internet traffic is now non-human, and it reports seeing a billion HTTP 402 responses on its network every day.

x402 is an attempt to finally give the internet a native way to pay. This guide covers what it is, how it works, who backs it, and whether there's a token involved.

The 402 "Payment Required" code has been in the HTTP standard since the 1990s. It was set aside "for future use", and x402 is the first big effort to actually use it.

x402 is an open payment standard built on top of HTTP, the protocol that moves data around the web. Coinbase launched it in May 2025 with partners including AWS, Anthropic, Circle and NEAR. It lets APIs, apps and AI agents pay for services in stablecoins as part of an ordinary web request.

x402 is not a blockchain and it is not a coin. It's a set of rules: message formats, headers and a request-and-response flow that any server or client can follow. Payments are mostly made in USDC, Circle's dollar-backed stablecoin, with Coinbase's Ethereum layer-2 network Base as the main settlement chain. The protocol works with any chain, though, and it now supports Solana, Polygon, Arbitrum and others, as well as traditional card payments.

On April 2, 2026, the Linux Foundation announced it was launching the x402 Foundation, with Coinbase contributing the protocol and companies including Microsoft, Google, Visa and Mastercard expressing initial support. The new x402 Foundation became fully operational on July 14, 2026, and now stewards the protocol under vendor-neutral governance.

We are excited to introduce x402–a new open source payments protocol built on HTTP rails.

Let’s break it down: pic.twitter.com/9PNI49NK7A

— Coinbase Developer Platform🛡️ (@CoinbaseDev) May 6, 2025

x402 works through a short handshake inside a normal web request:

Most of the onchain work is done by a facilitator, a service that verifies payments and settles them on the blockchain so sellers don't have to run their own crypto setup. On EVM chains, x402 uses EIP-3009, a standard that lets someone transfer tokens by signing a permission instead of sending a transaction themselves. This means the buyer doesn't need to hold ETH for gas.

x402's main pitch is very small payments made by machines. Settlement on Base takes seconds and costs well under a cent, so it makes sense to pay fractions of a cent for a single API call. There's no sign-up, no API key and no subscription. The agent just pays and gets what it asked for.

Adoption has grown quickly. Coinbase released x402 V2 in December 2025, which added wallet-based identity so agents don't have to pay again on every call, automatic service discovery and support for more chains. In May 2026 it added batch settlement, where agents hand sellers signed vouchers that can be settled in bulk. x402 had processed more than 196 million transactions across 12 chains as of November 1, 2026, settling more than $42 million in total, according to Fireblocks, which cited data from tracker agenteconomy.to. Coinbase says more than 97% of onchain agent transactions in Q2 2026 used x402.

It's worth noting, however, that more than half of x402's lifetime transactions came from a two-month frenzy in late 2025, when traders ran automated scripts to mint and sell PING, a token issued through x402, according to Fireblocks. Activity has since settled at a much lower level: in October 2026, x402 processed 5.1 million transactions worth about $274,000.

No. x402 is a protocol, not a cryptocurrency, and it has no official native token. Payments are made in existing stablecoins, mostly USDC. Speculative "x402" tokens have appeared on various chains, but none of them is issued or backed by Coinbase or the x402 Foundation. Be very wary of any token that claims to be the official x402 coin.

To use x402, an agent or app needs a wallet holding a supported stablecoin on a supported network, usually USDC on Base. USDC is widely available on major exchanges including Coinbase, Kraken and Binance. Coinbase lets users send USDC straight to Base. Developers can also use Coinbase Developer Platform wallets or Circle Wallets to give their agents funds, and set spending limits to keep those agents under control.

The x402 Foundation is growing fast. It had 40 member organizations when it became operational in July 2026, and its member list has since grown to 57, including Visa, Mastercard, American Express, PayPal, Google, Shopify, Circle, Stripe and AWS. Michael Hursta became the foundation's executive director on October 1, 2026.

x402 isn't the only option, though. The Agentic Commerce Protocol, an open standard developed by Stripe and OpenAI, and Visa's Trusted Agent Protocol are competing for a role in agent payments, even though Stripe and Visa are also premier members of the x402 Foundation.

Stripe has also co-developed the Machine Payments Protocol with payments blockchain Tempo. Like x402, MPP uses the HTTP 402 code to let agents pay for services, but adds session-based billing so agents can pay as they go rather than settling every request onchain.

Since June 2026, it has also supported x402 payments. The big open question is whether x402's huge transaction counts turn into steady, real commerce. If they do, the payment code that sat unused for 30 years could end up carrying much of the AI economy's payments.

That said, skipping foreplay is generally easier to do (and still get good results) with a long-term partner than a first-time hookup. “When you’ve been creating a larger sexual context in your relationship, you’re basically operating in that [state of foreplay] all the time,” says psychologist and relationship therapist Tracy Thomas, Ph.D. "If you’ve been together for a while, you should know them well enough to know when it’s okay to jump straight to the main event."

WHETHER YOU GO all out with accents and role-play or you simply concentrate a little more on erogenous zones, upping your foreplay game is only going to help you with every type of sexual interaction you have moving forward. “The build-up of arousal is a huge determinant in the overall height of the sensory experience,” says Reed. So go on, get a little more outta your foreplay.

#4968. Canton Coin Gets SEC Nod as Wall Street Tokenization Heats Up

Canton Coin Gets SEC Nod as Wall Street Tokenization Heats Up

The blockchain behind Wall Street’s newest tokenization service just got a regulatory nod for its native token.

The SEC’s Division of Corporation Finance said in a letter dated Tuesday that it views Canton Coin, the native token of the Canton Network, as a “digital commodity” under the agency’s March interpretation of how securities laws apply to crypto.

Agency staff also said they won’t recommend enforcement action if Canton’s validators and application providers mint the token programmatically without registering it.

The relief comes a week after the Depository Trust & Clearing Corp. launched a tokenization service that runs on Canton and DTCC’s own private network, letting firms convert DTC-held stocks and Treasuries into tokens. Morgan Stanley said Tuesday that it was among the first banks to use the service.

SEC staff pointed to two representations from Canton’s lawyers. The first is that the token derives its value from the network’s programmatic operation and from supply and demand, rather than from the managerial efforts of others.

The second is that the network has no “central party.” Canton Coin launched without an initial coin offering or premine, and tokens were initially earned only by running network infrastructure and applications, according to a statement from Canton. Canton’s request letter was dated Oct. 30, four days before staff responded.

The letter has limits, though, as it reflects only staff’s position on enforcement, not a legal conclusion, and rests on the facts Canton presented, the SEC noted.

“Financial institutions need confidence in both the technology they use and the economic model that supports it,” said Yuval Rooz, CEO of Digital Asset, which created the Canton Network.

Canton said the letter is the first relief of its kind since the SEC published its March interpretive release, which found that most crypto assets aren’t securities. It’s also the first since the agency’s August Regulation Crypto Assets proposal.

The decision fits a broader shift since the Clarity Act stalled in the Senate in September, with the SEC and CFTC taking the lead on crypto rules. SEC Chair Paul Atkins said in July the agency was ready to act if Congress didn’t, and the SEC recently proposed rules on how advisers and funds hold crypto.

Digital Asset raised $355 million earlier this year from Wall Street firms and a sovereign wealth fund.

BTC/USD four-hour chart. Source: Cointelegraph/TradingView

#4965. US Voters Back AI Data Center Restrictions in At Least 29 Communities

US Voters Back AI Data Center Restrictions in At Least 29 Communities

Voters in at least 29 U.S. jurisdictions backed bans or restrictions on data-center development Tuesday, according to The Washington Post, dealing a new setback to the massive infrastructure buildout behind artificial intelligence.

The measures ranged from outright prohibitions to requirements for additional public approval. Some were nonbinding advisory votes, meaning their passage does not automatically stop construction.

Ballotpedia tracked 41 data-center measures on November 3 ballots across 10 states. Twenty-eight were framed so that a yes vote would curb development, while 13 would permit or support projects, meaning rejecting one of the latter could also count as a defeat for developers.

Ohio produced some of the clearest results. All 10 charter measures backed by the grassroots group Conserve Ohio passed, according to the Post, and more than three-quarters of voters in Hubbard, Ohio, supported a ban on data centers.

The negative sentiment is so big that voters in Granville, Trenton, and Sunbury backed restrictions with margins of roughly 78% to 82%.

In Illinois, nearly 80% of voters in Joliet Township rejected an advisory proposal supporting AI data centers. In Janesville, Wisconsin, more than 60% approved a requirement for another public vote before major development at a former General Motors site linked to a proposed data center.

These facilities house the chips and servers used to train and run AI systems. Their appetite for electricity has turned proposed campuses into local fights over utility bills and grid capacity, while opponents also raise concerns about water use, noise and deals struck without public scrutiny.

AI companies are aware of the growing backlash and are already working to counter it. Some are stepping up lobbying efforts, while others are turning to public relations campaigns to reshape public perception. OpenAI, for instance, has sought to rebrand data centers as “compute campuses,” in an effort to distance the facilities from the negative connotations increasingly associated with them.

The votes arrived alongside victories by politicians promising tougher oversight of data centers. Democrat Gina Hinojosa defeated Republican Gov. Greg Abbott in Texas after campaigning for a moratorium on new projects, while Ohio's governor-elect Amy Acton also supports a pause and stricter rules.

Industry leaders have warned that blanket restrictions would put U.S. AI investment at risk. BlackRock CEO Larry Fink said last month that a broad moratorium could have serious economic consequences. "If we have a moratorium of some sort in this country, we will be in a recession," he said.

#4964. Circle’s ARC token sale lifts earnings as lower yields weigh on USDC income

Circle is looking to payments fees and future staking rewards to diversify revenue as its core stablecoin business faces pressure from lower yields and growing competition.

Circle’s expansion beyond stablecoins supplied the biggest boost to its third-quarter earnings, as the company recognized $182 million in revenue from its ARC token presale, helping offset falling income from the reserves backing its flagship stablecoin, USDC.

The company reported diluted earnings per share of $0.84, up from $0.64 a year earlier and double analysts’ expectations of $0.42. Total revenue and reserve income rose 17% to $865 million. Without the ARC token sale, however, revenue would have fallen from a year earlier.

Circle shares were down 0.84% at $74.59 at 4:05 pm UTC on Nov. 4, according to Yahoo Finance.

The results capture a transition for Circle. Its established stablecoin business earns returns on the reserves backing USDC, tying income to interest rates and the amount of the token outstanding. Its newer businesses aim to earn from the payments and financial activity those digital dollars support.

Circle launched its payments network in May 2025 to connect financial institutions settling cross-border payments with stablecoins. Arc, its blockchain for financial applications, went live on public mainnet on Sept. 16, 2026. Its native token, ARC, is intended to support network security and governance as Arc moves toward proof of stake in 2027, while transaction fees are paid in USDC.

That contribution arrived as lower reserve yields outweighed growth in USDC balances. Average circulation increased 8% year over year, but the reserve return fell 61 basis points to 3.5%. Reserve income declined 8% to $657.6 million.

Interest rates were lower than a year earlier, despite a Federal Reserve rate hike in September. The Fed raised its benchmark range by a quarter percentage point to 3.75%–4% in September, although that remained below the 4%–4.25% range set a year earlier. Circle’s reserve return also edged up from 3.48% in the second quarter, but the earnings pressure reflected the lower year-over-year yield.

USDC ended September at $73.9 billion in circulation, while its share of the stablecoin market fell 1.83 percentage points to 27%.

Competition is also expanding. Open Standard’s Open USD was live on Solana by Sept. 30, with Coinbase, Mastercard, Shopify, Stripe and Visa as founding partners. The stablecoin model distributes reserve earnings to partners after a management fee, giving businesses another option for accessing stablecoins and sharing their economics.

Related: ARK buys $17M in Coinbase, Circle shares while trimming Bullish

ARC changed Circle’s earnings picture. Excluding ARC presale revenue, total revenue and reserve income were $683 million, down roughly 8% year over year. Reported adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization rose 85% to $317 million. Without the ARC contribution, adjusted EBITDA was approximately $135 million, down 21% from $171 million a year earlier.

Circle anticipates staking rewards after Arc transitions to proof of stake, which it targets for 2027. That would create another source of revenue, although it remains dependent on the network’s development.

Circle is also beginning to charge for some payments-network activity. Chief Financial Officer Jeremy Fox-Geen said on the earnings call that the company expected material CPN revenue in 2027. CEO Jeremy Allaire described Circle’s strategy as building connected networks, with growth in stablecoins, payments and blockchain applications reinforcing one another.

“This is not just about payments, and it is not just about digital dollars. It is about multiple compounding flywheels on one platform. Every new member, every new builder, and every new asset makes the whole platform more valuable,” Allaire said.

The company is also adjusting its workforce size. The earnings disclosures included a reduction affecting approximately 5% of employees, which Circle said was intended to align its organization with its operating model and strategic priorities.

#4962. US Bank Regulator Issues First AI Framework as Banks Battle Rogue Agents

US Bank Regulator Issues First AI Framework as Banks Battle Rogue Agents

The U.S. Office of the Comptroller of the Currency issued its first supervisory framework for artificial intelligence in banking today, telling examiners to focus less on how powerful an AI system is and more on what happens when it makes a mistake.

The OCC's framework addresses that very concern, although the regulator has not linked its issuance to those specific incidents.

In a section covering operational resilience, the agency warns that external agents operated by customers, fintechs, or other businesses could create risks for banks even when the banks don't control those systems.

It suggests examiners consider measures including identity verification, delegated permissions, transaction limits, monitoring, and the ability to suspend or revoke access.

The guidelines aim to tackle the dilemma of how to let customers use AI assistants to manage their finances without giving autonomous software unrestricted access to accounts that banks are already facing.

The new OCC guidance arrives less than two weeks after reports that major banks were discovering unauthorized third-party AI agents operating inside customer accounts, sometimes taking actions that appeared to go beyond users' instructions.

Morgan Stanley was blocking outside agents, while JPMorgan, Bank of America, and Citi were restricting access, according to an Axios report on October 22. Bank executives described the situation as "horrifying," raising questions about who controls an autonomous agent once it has access to someone's money.

The OCC's framework makes a distinction between an AI system's technical sophistication and the damage it could cause.

A simple agent authorized to initiate financial transactions may present greater risk than a more advanced model limited to writing internal documents, the regulator said.

Under the 22-page framework, examiners can consider the consequences of failure, whether an action can be reversed, and how much control humans retain over the system.

The agency outlines four illustrative risk tiers. Fully autonomous payment agents and real-time credit decision systems fall into the highest category, while basic internal productivity tools occupy the lowest.

Higher-risk systems may warrant tighter restrictions on autonomy, independent testing, and human approval before consequential actions can proceed.

The framework also addresses AI systems that continue operating while making harmful decisions. Suggested safeguards include circuit breakers, rate limits, restricted permissions, and emergency shutdown controls.

Those considerations are especially relevant to the outside agents described in Axios's earlier reporting, where banks struggled to distinguish legitimate customer-authorized software from systems operating beyond their intended permissions.

The OCC stressed that the document does not create new regulatory requirements or enforceable standards for banks.

Instead, it gives examiners a way to evaluate AI within existing operational, compliance, and cybersecurity risk categories. Banks are not required to establish a separate AI risk category.

The document uses the mandatory term "Super Intelligence," which the Donald Trump administration insists on using in place of “artificial intelligence,” but its scope includes conventional AI and “autonomous agents” rather than only hypothetical systems with intelligence beyond human capabilities.

The framework covers AI used in payments, fraud detection, customer service, and other banking operations. It also extends its risk considerations to external AI agents connecting through bank channels.

The OCC said it intends to update the guidance as the technology develops. For now, its illustrative risk scale puts autonomous payment agents in the highest-risk category, regardless of how well they perform in testing.

#4961. Coinbase Says 99% of Customer Crypto Is Offline and Out of Hackers’ Reach

Coinbase Says 99% of Customer Crypto Is Offline and Out of Hackers’ Reach

Coinbase is moving to reassure customers that what happened at Binance can’t easily happen there.

In a blog post Wednesday, the U.S. exchange said it wasn’t affected by last week’s hack of Binance, in which attackers drained about $6.4 billion from the rival exchange’s hot wallets.

Coinbase said it keeps more than 99% of customer assets in cold storage, meaning offline wallets that can’t be reached over the internet. It moves funds into internet-connected hot wallets only as customers need them.

The Binance incident “has understandably left customers asking” how Coinbase guards against a similar attack, the post said.

Coinbase laid out several layers of protection. Private keys for its cold wallets are split into pieces and never fully reassembled, even when signing transactions, and no single employee has standing access to the hot-wallet signing service. Strict limits cap how fast funds can move from cold to hot wallets, and unusual withdrawal spikes are held until staff review them. The exchange can also trigger “kill switches” that take signing keys offline entirely.

The company said it assumes employees will be targeted by phishing, so its controls are designed so that a stolen password, session or device can’t be used on its own, and sensitive actions require multiple approvals.

That focus on insiders carries weight for Coinbase. In May 2025, it disclosed that criminals had bribed overseas customer support agents to steal personal data on less than 1% of its monthly transacting users, then demanded a $20 million ransom that Coinbase refused to pay.

Last week, Coinbase said a security incident at analytics vendor Amplitude exposed contact data for a “small subset” of customers, though funds weren’t affected.

Coinbase also said customer assets are held 1:1 and aren’t lent out unless customers direct it to, and it reported $8.6 billion in cash and cash equivalents and $377 billion in assets on its platform as of Sept. 30. It flagged artificial intelligence as a fast-changing threat and pledged further security investment.

The Binance theft, which the FBI has attributed to North Korea’s TraderTraitor group, has rattled the industry. Blockchain analytics firm Elliptic found that the laundering spanned about 12,000 wallets in under four days, while stablecoin issuers froze about 44% of the haul.

Separately, Brazil’s Mercado Bitcoin halted withdrawals after a $40 million hack, and French bank Crédit Mutuel Arkéa recently confirmed a cyberattack that led to a $133 million crypto theft.
