#4960. The Men's Health 30-Day Workout Challenge

You can get moving to get in shape—but you have to commit.

SOMETIMES, THE MOST challenging part of working out is knowing what to do.

You might not have a definite goal in mind other than that you want to get moving, or that you want to look different by the time the summer season rolls around. That's okay. Not everyone is wired to constantly aspire to build muscle or run fast, following a detailed program designed to elicit peak performance and radical transformation. Just wanting to get active to live a healthier life is a worthy goal, especially if you're taking your first steps to make fitness part of your daily routine or recommitting to exercise after a break. But you have to be ready to commit to your new routine for it to be effective.

That's what the Men's Health 30-Day Workout Challenge is all about: doing something every day. Rather than limiting the focus to just one specific body part, muscle group, or type of exercise, as we have with our previous challenges, we're more interested in establishing healthy habits and introducing you to common exercise protocols that will be useful for the rest of your fitness life. Beginners can take the challenge and hold their own, while fitness pros can still push themselves to work to their full potential using these routines.

The plan is simple. You'll take on a new workout routine every day—but there will be some consistencies so you won't be constantly thrown into the deep end if you don't have a ton of experience. All of the workouts will be comprised out of a pool of just 11 exercises, which you can see demonstrated below by the program's architect, Men's Health fitness director Ebenezer Samuel, C.S.C.S.

Each week of the program will be focused on a different workout scheme, so you can get the swing of the structure and get comfortable within each protocol. The calendar will also be structured in a manner that allows you to flex all of your muscles, both figuratively and literally; Day 1 of each week will be focused on Mobility and Core, Day 2 is Upper Push/Pull, Day 3 is Lower body, Day 4 is a Mobility focus, Day 5 is Upper Push/Pull, Day 6 is an Upper/Lower/Core split, and Day 7 is all Core.

All you have to do is buy in. By Day 30 (or the bonus Day 31, if you're game), at the very least you'll have completed this challenge. If you put in max effort, you'll also be moving better, feeling fitter, and looking different than you did on Day 1.

For each interval workout, work for 40 seconds, then take 20 seconds off unless otherwise stated. Repeat for 2 rounds. The goal: Work for quality reps for each move.

• Minute 1: Spiderman Lunge to T-Spine• Minute 2: Hollow Rock• Minute 3: Plank Shoulder Tap• Minute 4: Mountain Climber

Work for 40:20 first time through, then 50:10

You know the exercises you’re working with. Now things get fun. This week, we introduce you to AMRAPS, as many rounds as possible. You’ll work for 8 minutes in each workout this week, and your goal is to complete as many rounds of the circuit we lay out for you as possible. Don’t lose your focus on quality reps, but do aim to keep score.

This week you’ll learn the beauty of the EMOM, every minute on the minute. You’ll once again work for 8 minutes, completing the entire sequence of moves we give you every minute. Your goal: Finish all the work as quickly as possible (while still maintaining good form!) so you can rest until the start of the next minute.

This last week, you’ll amp the intensity one final time, mastering the EMOM+1. That means the first minute of your workout may be easy, but the last minute will be ultra-challenging as you race against the clock to hit all your reps.

#4959. Riot shares surge on $28B AI lease as Hut 8 sinks on revenue miss

Bitcoin miner Riot sold BTC to finance its AI expansion, while Hut 8 reported falling revenue and rising costs before its major data center leases generate income.

Shares of Bitcoin miner Riot Platforms surged more than 13% Wednesday after the company announced a $28 billion AI data center lease, its largest agreement to date as it accelerates its expansion into artificial intelligence infrastructure.

The 15-year agreement covers 910 megawatts at its Corsicana, Texas, facility, bringing its total future contracted data center revenue to approximately $38 billion across three leases signed this year. The agreement represents a major step in Riot’s AI infrastructure strategy as it moves away from relying primarily on Bitcoin mining revenue.

“A year ago, our cash flow depended on the price of Bitcoin, and now, at full deployment of currently executed deals, nearly all of our expected cash flow will be generated from long-term leases with leading players in the AI ecosystem,” CEO Jason Les said during Wednesday’s earnings call.

According to Riot’s third-quarter earnings release, Riot will provide land and power infrastructure originally developed for Bitcoin mining, while the AI tenant, identified as Anthropic in the company’s quarterly filing, will own and operate the computing equipment and cover operating expenses.

Riot estimates the project’s total capital costs at $5.5 billion, financed primarily through debt backed by lease payments, although financing has not yet been secured. The first phase is expected to be delivered in September 2027, with rental payments beginning within 60 days of delivery.

Riot also reported third-quarter revenue of $181.4 million, up just 1% year over year, and sold 10,599 BTC during the quarter to help finance its data center expansion. During Wednesday’s earnings call, CEO Jason Les said Bitcoin mining at Corsicana could begin winding down in summer 2027.

Related: Bitcoin miners face widening AI financing costs as lenders scrutinize deals

Meanwhile, shares of fellow Bitcoin miner Hut 8 plunged more than 22% at one point on Wednesday after the company reported weaker-than-expected quarterly revenue amid its transition toward AI infrastructure.

Third-quarter revenue fell 15% to $71.1 million, missing analysts’ estimates of $75.8 million, according to MarketBeat data. During Wednesday’s earnings call, CFO Sean Glennan attributed the decline partly to asset sales and lower revenue per Bitcoin mined, while cash administrative expenses also rose year over year.

“The legacy revenue base is no longer the main growth driver, while the larger contracted infrastructure revenues remain tied to delivery milestones,” Glennan said.

CEO Asher Genoot also pointed to the complexity and expense of traditional AI data center construction as the company looks to develop projects on a larger scale.

“Many of today’s data centers resemble NASA rocket programs—remarkable engineering, but complex, expensive, and slow to deliver,” Genoot said.

According to the company’s third-quarter earnings presentation, Hut 8 has already secured $7.5 billion in project bonds to fully finance two major AI data center developments, although neither has begun generating lease revenue.

#4958. Google Tells Schools They Can Reject AI—But They'll Lose Search, Too

Google Tells Schools They Can Reject AI—But They'll Lose Search, Too

Google is giving schools more freedom to reject artificial intelligence—provided they're also willing to give up Google Search, which is arguably what everybody in the world uses Google for.

The company announced Tuesday that its AI products will be disabled by default for K-12 student accounts unless administrators explicitly enable them. But schools that want to ban AI entirely will also have to disable Google's search engine, a tool millions of students rely on for everyday research.

Chris Phillips, Google's vice president and general manager of education, tried to justify the reasoning during a meeting with educators.

“Search and AI are deeply integrated,” he said according to Education Week. Schools uncomfortable with AI can choose not to enable Search at all.

Google says administrators will be able to activate or disable Gemini, Gemini Notebook, Gemini in Classroom, and Search individually. That means schools can block the standalone chatbots while keeping Search available, but they cannot remove AI from Search itself.

For teachers who want students to research sources without receiving AI-generated answers, the company is effectively offering two options: accept its AI-powered search or find another search engine.

The announcement is already drawing accusations that Google is using its dominance in education to push a technology many schools are still trying to understand, regulate or restrict.

Google says the changes are intended to address concerns that AI chatbots can undermine learning by giving students ready-made answers instead of encouraging them to solve problems themselves.

Beginning in early 2027, schools that enable Search will be offered versions with conventional AI features, such as AI Overviews and AI Mode, or the education-focused Guided Learning experience, according to The New York Times.

Neither option provides the traditional search experience without generative AI.

New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani accused the company of “trying to use its monopoly power to force AI into our public schools,” according to CBS News.

Former Federal Trade Commission chair Lina Khan was equally critical, accusing Google of using its search monopoly to coerce schools into adopting AI.

The criticism comes as some of America's largest school districts are actively restricting generative AI. New York City, for example, imposed a one-year moratorium on student-facing generative AI through eighth grade, covering nearly 600,000 students, while Los Angeles has also restricted the technology.

Los Angeles officials had specifically hoped to preserve Google Search while removing AI-generated summaries. Under the newly announced controls, that won't be an option.

Google's position also arrives amid continuing antitrust scrutiny. A federal court ruled in 2024 that Google unlawfully monopolized the U.S. search market, and the company has faced separate legal challenges over its decision to integrate AI summaries into search results.

School administrators are expected to receive the new controls in early 2027. They will be able to decide which AI products students can access, and even apply different settings by grade level. A Google Search option without AI, however, is not among the choices.

That said, for schools that want to, ahem, unofficially ban AI without losing Google search, our guide may provide a quick solution. It takes 5 minutes and lasts forever.

#4957. ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

ChatGPT Is Getting a GIF Maker Powered by OpenAI's Unannounced Images 2.6 Model

The internet may soon have an unlimited supply of AI-generated dancing cats, courtesy of ChatGPT.

OpenAI is rolling out a feature that lets users generate animated GIFs directly inside ChatGPT, according to The Verge. The feature reportedly comes powered by its new image-generation model called Images 2.6, which OpenAI has yet to formally introduce through its usual product announcements.

Users can describe an animation in plain English or upload an existing photo and ask ChatGPT to bring it to life. Once the GIF is generated, they can adjust its playback speed, inspect individual frames, make edits, and share the finished animation.

OpenAI demonstrated the capability with a simple request: “can you make a gif of a cat dancing?” The resulting interface reportedly includes controls to change animation speed and examine the GIF frame by frame.

We tried generating GIFs using ChatGPT, but the feature was not available on our accounts at the time of testing. In our interaction, the model generates a series of frames, but not the animation.

The rollout appears to be gradual, so users may have to keep asking for their dancing cats a little longer.

The GIF maker is reportedly part of a broader Images 2.6 update that promises faster image generation. The Verge reports that OpenAI is bringing the model to ChatGPT and Codex users across web, desktop, and mobile platforms.

Developers are also getting two new API models, GPT-Image-2.6 Flare and GPT-Image-2.6 Sunburst, designed for faster generation and higher-quality results, respectively. OpenAI has not yet published a standalone announcement detailing the new model's capabilities, and we could not independently test its performance.

The update follows the September release of ChatGPT Images 2.5, which introduced sharper details, more precise editing, and generation speeds up to 50% faster than its predecessor.

In our previous testing, Images 2.5 went head-to-head with Google's Nano Banana 2 across six categories, with each model winning three. OpenAI's model delivered particularly impressive illustration quality and improved on the oversharpening problems that affected earlier versions, although it still made mistakes when rendering text.

The move into GIF generation also comes months after OpenAI shut down Sora, its standalone AI video-generation app, in March. Animated GIFs are considerably simpler than the videos Sora generated, but the new feature brings a form of motion generation directly into ChatGPT without requiring a separate application.

OpenAI has not provided a specific date for when every ChatGPT account will receive GIF generation.

#4956. Trump-linked tokens lead crypto losses after Democrats sweep in the US midterms

The official Trump and World Liberty Financial tokens were the two biggest losers among the top 100 cryptocurrencies on Wednesday, even as Bitcoin and Ether rallied.

Two crypto tokens linked to US President Donald Trump led losses among the top 100 digital assets on Wednesday following the US midterm elections, raising questions about their future as the broader crypto market rallied.

Official Trump (TRUMP) fell 17% over 24 hours to $1.33, while World Liberty Financial (WLFI) dropped over 7% to $0.047, according to CoinMarketCap data from around 5:32 pm UTC. TRUMP is the president’s official memecoin, launched in January 2025, while WLFI is the governance token of World Liberty Financial, a Trump family-backed decentralized finance (DeFi) project launched in 2024.

The declines followed a wave of Democratic victories, including surprise wins in Texas, in Tuesday’s midterm elections, with the party winning control of both the House and Senate from Republicans.

The results could not only make it harder for Trump to push forward his crypto plans but also bring more scrutiny to his family’s crypto businesses. Analysts say the losses also raise questions about whether political sentiment alone can fuel demand for Trump-linked tokens.

Bitcoin (BTC) rose more than 4% over 24 hours to around $80,500, while Ether (ETH) climbed 6% to $2,433, according to CoinMarketCap data. Other major cryptocurrencies also posted gains, with BNB rising 3%, XRP climbing 2.6% and Solana (SOL) gaining 2.5%. The overall crypto market rose 3.4% to $2.69 trillion.

Meanwhile, the losses extended to other Trump family-linked tokens, such as the MELANIA memecoin, associated with First Lady Melania Trump. MELANIA dropped nearly 13% over 24 hours to around $0.066.

A separate token named after Vice President JD Vance (VANCE) also dropped nearly 12%, although it has no confirmed official connection to the vice president.

The losses were further reflected across the broader political memecoin market, with the sector’s total market capitalization falling more than 16% on the day to $477 million. Trading volume jumped 123% to $437 million, per CoinMarketCap data.

Related: US midterm elections 2026 as it happened: Democrats win control of Congress

Benjamin Sarquis Peillard, founder and CEO of Cap, a DeFi platform focused on stablecoin yields, told Cointelegraph that investors appear to be treating politically linked tokens differently from other cryptocurrencies.

“Bitcoin and other established assets respond to a combination of liquidity, institutional flows and macroeconomic conditions, while politically linked tokens can be more sensitive to shifts in expectations around Washington,” he said. “Even as the broader market recovers, those tokens may face selling pressure if investors begin reassessing their risk exposure.”

And while the selloff could be indicative of a short-term reaction to the election results, Peillard said the “longer-term question is what supports demand for these tokens beyond their association with a political figure.”

Beyond the market reaction, the election results also raise questions about the future of US crypto legislation, including the currently stalled Digital Asset Market Clarity Act, commonly referred to as the CLARITY Act.

The bill, which aims to establish a regulatory framework for digital assets, failed to advance in the Senate earlier this year after falling short of the 60 votes required. Democrats were pushing for stronger ethical provisions and safeguards against conflicts of interest involving Trump’s crypto ventures — four Republicans also opposed advancing the legislation.

“The midterm results introduce a new layer of regulatory uncertainty, particularly around the pace and direction of digital asset legislation,” Peillard said. “For politically linked tokens, that uncertainty can have a more immediate impact because investor sentiment is partly tied to expectations around the political environment.”

While the crypto sector continues to have support — and opposition — from lawmakers in both parties, it remains to be seen how the election results will affect crypto legislation.

Peillard said that for the broader industry, the “more important question is whether Congress can establish a durable regulatory framework that provides clarity regardless of which party holds power.”

#4955. Why Bitcoin's Next Soft Fork Could Feel Like a Practice Run

Why Bitcoin's Next Soft Fork Could Feel Like a Practice Run

Four flaws in Bitcoin’s consensus rules that have lingered for years.

Issues like quantum resistance may command attention, but a practical cleanup is what many experts think Bitcoin’s next soft fork is likely to address, spanning fixes for vulnerabilities in the network’s difficulty adjustment mechanism to denial-of-service vectors among miners.

Proposed in 2019 and known as the Great Consensus Cleanup (BIP-54), the upgrade seeks to address the “cruft” in Bitcoin’s consensus design through changes that have historically been sidelined, as described by long-time Bitcoin developer and BIP-54 co-author Matt Corallo.

“There’s a laundry list of things we know of that we really need to clean up,” he said during a recent appearance on MARA Foundation TV. “All of these issues are real concerns that could impact Bitcoin negatively, but they’re not like, ‘Oh, the sky is falling.’”

Although a formal timeline for the BIP’s undertaking hasn’t been established, Corallo thinks there’s an 80% chance that the Great Consensus Cleanup is up next for the network. During his talk with Head of MARA Foundation Isabel Foxen Duke, he also called it a practical exercise for Bitcoin developers who haven’t yet been through a soft fork.

At its core, Corallo said BIP-54 “fixes some stuff that everyone agrees are problems,” while targeting four specific vulnerabilities:

Still, not everyone views a maintenance-focused soft fork as a major win. On X, Paul Sztorc, co-founder and CEO of LayerTwo Labs, called BIP-54’s potential activation “kind of depressing, since it is a janitorial maintenance fork that doesn't do anything.”

Surveying developers at TABconf in Atlanta, Sztorc noted that Bitcoin’s “best and brightest” predominantly expect the Great Consensus Cleanup to be Bitcoin’s next soft fork anyway, with the earliest estimate in September 2027, and most pointing to 2028 or later.

Following the activation of Taproot in 2021, Bitcoin’s community has faced years of gridlock surrounding protocol changes. This friction is frequently framed around “ossification,” with broader protocol changes like OP_CAT (BIP-347) and OP_CHECKTEMPLATEVERIFY (BIP-119) struggling to reach ecosystem-wide consensus.

According to Corallo, soft forks have always involved overcoming technical hurdles. But more recently, he’s observed political and social friction that’s become just as relevant.

The Bitcoin community spent months debating how Taproot should be activated, with arguments pitting miner-signaling thresholds against mandatory user flag days. Corallo argues that BIP-54 can establish a deployment playbook for future soft forks.

“In every recent soft fork, there’s also been a bunch of drama about activation mechanisms,” he said. “So hopefully we could do a soft fork to go through that drama, and then copy and paste and reuse it and head off some of that drama [for future soft forks].”

In that sense, BIP-54 is just as much about proving Bitcoin can still agree on upgrades at the social layer as it is about actual code cleanup.

André Beganski is a Senior Content Manager at MARA Foundation, producing content on topics including the intersection of quantum computing and cryptography. He has previously worked for Decrypt.

#4954. The 7 Best LED Face Masks for Men in 2026, According to Experts

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

For battling blemishes and signs of aging, there are few better at-home tools.

AS SOMEONE RAISED on slasher horror movies, I was always going to gravitate towards LED face masks. But while their creepy Jason Voorhees-at-a-rave look is what initially grabbed my attention, the skincare benefits are what sold me on the products. LED face masks combine the firming power of anti-aging products, the depuffing of eye cream, the energizing boost of moisturizers, and the breakout-fighting strength of acne washes—making them one of the most versatile grooming tools out there.

LED masks use specific wavelengths of light to penetrate the skin and trigger cellular changes, according to dermatologist Dr. Hannah Kopelman, MD. This can include stimulating collagen production to reduce fine lines and support firmer skin, as well as treating acne-causing bacteria and calming inflammation to promote a clearer complexion. Some devices offer all of these benefits, while others are laser-focused (get it?) on just one or two. The key is to know what each is capable of and what service you’re looking for.

Dermatologist Dr. Brian Toy, MD, warns that the market is flooded with LED face masks that vary greatly in quality and effectiveness, so it’s best to start your search as informed as possible. To temper the confusion, I went ahead and used myself, along with fellow Men’s Health writers and editors, as guinea pigs. This way, you’ll know what works and what is merely an overpriced Halloween mask.

We gave Shark Beauty CryoGlow a 2025 Men’s Health Grooming Award barely a month after it was released; that’s how great this LED mask is. It offers four separate treatment options that cover anything you could possibly want out of an LED face mask. The Better Aging setting uses red and infrared light to reduce wrinkles and firm skin, while Skin Clearing mode combines blue, red, and infrared light to fight blemishes and even tone.

The Skin Sustain maintains glow with a quick mix of all three LED wavelengths, and Under-Eye Revive uses cryotherapy-inspired cooling to depuff and tighten the under-eye area. You can also use this under-eye cooling ability combined with the other treatments, which roll out in three increasing levels of chill. Each is between four and eight minutes, meaning you don’t need to dedicate much time to combat whatever issue you’re after. Under-Eye Revive, you can do up to 15 minutes if you’d like, but you only need five to be effective.

Dr. Peterson Pierre, MD, FAAD, likes that this device uses red, blue, and infrared lights so that you can get a full smorgasbord of benefits, like combating acne, fighting signs of aging, reducing inflammation, and helping regulate oil production. He also digs the cooling feature and three levels of intensity. I’ve been using my CryoGlow for months, and have seen a noticeable change in the smoothness and clearness of my skin, as well as reduced redness, using The Better Aging and Skin Clearing settings. But where I saw the most immediate results was with eye puffiness.

If I’m ever running on limited sleep or recovering from a night out, I pop the mask on, and after a few minutes of Under-Eye Revive mode, I look like I’ve actually slept. The light from this device doesn’t feel glaring when you’re wearing it, so I was able to successfully walk around and multitask during my treatments.

Plus, as someone who is generally sensitive to light, it also feels a lot less intense than other options on this list, and never inspired headaches of any kind. Not to mention, it’s easy to adjust and fits my face perfectly. I only have a few nitpicks. Given the expansive options and customizability of this mask, there is a handheld controller attached to it to help you navigate. While this makes firing up the device super easy and you don’t need to rely on an app, the controller can be a bit awkward to deal with while you’re in treatment.

You can’t put it in your pocket without accidentally changing the setting, so you have to hold it, which limits the multitasking you’re able to do. Also, the mask is bulkier than its main competition for best overall, the Dr. Dennis Gross Face Mask. But when you consider that the CryoGlow is also the cheapest full-face option on this list, it’s a fair trade-off.

Read More: Shark CryoGlow LED Face Mask Review

Dr. Kopelman has been recommending this LED mask to patients for years. She likes that it is FDA-cleared and offers both red and blue light; however, what she likes most about it is that it consistently delivers results. In terms of effectiveness, it’s equal to the CryoGlow. The only reason it just missed out on the top spot is that it’s over $100 more expensive and offers fewer additional treatments, like under-eye cooling. Beyond that, I find it just as successful at battling blemishes and signs of aging as the Shark Beauty.

Plus, the treatments are shorter, and you only need to wear this for three minutes a day to see results. Unlike many other options, like the CurrentBody or FAQ 202, which are made of more flexible material you can mold to your face, this design is rigid. While that may seem like a drawback, I loved it. Of all the LED masks on this list, I found Dr. Dennis Gross to be the sturdiest, and it fit my face perfectly. It’s slim, without feeling flimsy, and doesn’t weigh down my face like other similarly sturdy masks that tend to be a bit heavier, such as the Theraface Mask.

That being said, I possess an incredibly average build and face shape, so if you fall outside of those parameters, the singular and unadaptable design of this mask might not be as perfect a fit. Unlike the CryoGlow, it’s wireless when you’re using it and can be conveniently charged with any USB-C charger. This helps when traveling, though the solid design poses some challenges if you’re tight on storage space.

Similar to the Dr. Dennis Gross mask, this device is crafted with a hard, contoured shell; however, it’s far less slim. But it trades off this added bulkiness with its vibrating ability, which soothes facial tension and boosts blood flow around the eyes, brows, and scalp. It offers red, blue, and infrared light therapy to target acne, improve skin tone, and increase collagen production.

You can choose between three- or nine-minute treatment modes, though we found the longer sessions to be more effective. Sure, this mask can feel a bit heavy or bulky on the face, but the results are legit. In fact, some effects can be seen immediately, and they only increase from there. “After the very first time I used it, my skin felt softer, and my pores looked smaller,” says Senior Gear and Commerce Editor Ryan Brower.

“Over time, my skin tone looks more even, and my skin appears to be firmer, smoother, and less puffy.” He adds that the massaging, vibrating feature is relaxing, although it can feel ticklish or even chill-inducing at times. Since it sits close to your ears, it can also get a bit loud, especially when multitasking. Still, it’s really easy to switch between settings, turn on and off, and choose the massage style you want.

If you’re solely looking to combat puffiness, dark circles, and wrinkles, Lumin is the way to go. Sure, it looks straight out of an abandoned Lone Ranger In Space reboot, but there is no device better at rejuvenating the skin around the eyes than this mask. One tester saw noticeable improvements after days of use with this device. “I naturally have darker circles, but after consistent daily use for about a week, I saw a noticeable difference in the brightness and overall alertness of my eye area,” she says. The mask stays in place well, and the soft silicone padding makes it comfortable throughout each session.

Its adjustable design also ensures it fits all face shapes and sizes with ease. The same tester also saw a brighter tone and smoother skin after a couple of weeks of daily use. “While I don’t personally have under-eye wrinkles, I can see how this would be especially helpful for someone who does or for anyone who spends a lot of time in the sun,” she says. Not to mention, the Luminator is quick and easy to use—just three minutes a day with an auto timer.

Our testers found that this FAQ mask makes a strong impression right out of the box with its ultra-thin, flexible design, which feels more like a high-end sheet mask than a tech device. You’ll need to download the app to get the most out of this tool, however. There’s a single button on the mask, but without the app, we found that it was nearly impossible to remember its function or how to power it off.

Once the app is set up and the mask is synced, navigating the features becomes straightforward—just select your desired treatment and that’s it. Despite being covered in tiny LED lights, the mask feels surprisingly lightweight and comfortable. During the 15-minute session, testers reported no heat or noticeable sensation on the skin and even forgot it was on while doing other tasks, such as reading. The mask stays comfortably in place, even when looking down at a book or phone.

We also appreciated the included cleaning spray. For anyone with sensitive or breakout-prone skin, keeping the mask clean is essential, and the spray makes that simple. After each use, we cleaned and dried the mask before placing it in its pouch, which securely holds the device and makes storage hassle-free. One minor concern was the split at the bridge of the nose—designed for flexibility—which left one tester wondering if that area was receiving full treatment. Still, this didn’t deter them from continuing to use it.

Women’s Health fitness contributor Nicolette Accardi says that the Qure Pro LED Mask has taken her nightly skincare regimen to the next level. After a month of use, she saw a dramatic difference in her skin’s appearance. She used to struggle with oily skin and frequent breakouts, but since regularly using the pink LED light setting, she says it has significantly reduced her sebum production. “I'm also approaching 30 (yikes), so I've been using the red LED light setting as well to help combat fine lines and reverse skin aging,” Accardi says.

Each session runs for three minutes and automatically shuts off when it’s done, so you never have to worry about timing it yourself. It’s perfect for anyone who wants results without the hassle. It’s one of the easiest, most low-maintenance skincare tools out there at a relatively low cost. That’s not to say $380 is cheap, but when it comes to legit, effective LED masks, you won’t find many other options under $400. It’s really just this and the CryoGlow.

If the more rigid masks, like CryoGlow, Qure, and Dr. Dennis Gross, are too bulky for you, but the FAQ is too small for your face, Currentbody will be your best option. This mask offers the most flexibility, with a silicone design that easily molds to the contours of any face, ensuring full coverage. This is as long as you tighten the straps. I’ve been lazy a few times with that, and it will start slipping off your face mid-treatment. In addition to the flexible fit, this mask feels super gentle on the face.

One of our testers used it only days after getting eye surgery with no issue. Didn’t bother her eyes one bit. That being said, it takes up to six hours for a full charge, which can be a bit annoying. This is the longest charge time on this list by a few hours, to be clear. I’m the type of person who pushes off charging a device until I absolutely have to, so this long charge time usually results in me skipping a day of treatment. But if you’re a better planner (to be fair, it’s a low bar compared to me), it’ll be less of an issue.

Depending on what skin issues or concerns you’re looking to address with your LED face mask, you’ll want to focus on which treatments it offers. This is driven by the color of light the device uses, which dictates its benefits. Some on this list offer multiple services, while others zero in on one. These are the common options:

While some are comfortable and allow for multitasking, there is no getting around the fact that you’re wearing a large light on your face. As a long-time migraine sufferer, having a light this close to my eyes for too long can be a trigger. But even if you’re perfectly fine with prolonged light exposure, you’ll want to know what the time commitment is before buying LED face masks, as they’re most effective when used regularly. Some are as long as 25 minutes, while others can be as short as three minutes. Keep in mind that not all are meant for everyday use; some (especially the ones with longer treatment lengths) may only need to be used a few times a week for max efficiency.

When shopping for an LED face mask, choosing a device that’s FDA-cleared is essential, says Dr. Kopelman. This ensures the product meets fundamental safety standards for consumer use. To be clear, however, FDA-cleared is not the same as FDA-approved—the latter involves extensive clinical trials and rigorous testing. Currently, no LED face masks on the market have achieved FDA approval.

For now, stick with FDA-cleared options, which means the device is proven to be as safe and effective as another legally marketed product. You might also see terms like FDA-registered or FDA-listed, but these simply indicate that the manufacturer has notified the FDA of the product's existence—there’s no review or evaluation involved. Because of that uncertainty, you won’t see any of those on this list.

There are a lot of LED face masks out there, and like any trendy tool, not all are created equal. So, before putting any to the test, I consulted with a panel of dermatologists on which devices were legit and which they recommend. They also advised me on what to look for in a quality LED face mask and the brands to focus on. Based on this intel, I, along with other Men’s Health writers and editors, put several masks to the test, evaluating each device’s effectiveness, ease of use, and how comfortable it was to wear.

As many LED face masks do not offer immediate results, we used each one for at least one month to ensure enough time was given to gain any possible benefits. Following this extensive research and testing, I landed on these seven LED face masks as the best for men in 2026.

The dermatologists I spoke to for this story were clear on this: you should be cleaning your device after every use. If you don’t, bacteria, oil, and product residue can build up on the surface and transfer back onto your skin, causing irritation or breakouts, which may ironically be what you were hoping to minimize by using an LED mask in the first place.

Dr. Kopelman says that LED masks should always be used on freshly cleansed, dry skin to ensure the light penetrates as effectively as possible. After the session, apply your serums and moisturizers—doing it beforehand can block the light and reduce its benefits. A hydrating serum like hyaluronic acid or a peptide-rich cream works especially well to complement the collagen boost from the treatment, Dr. Kopelman says.

According to Dr. Toy, be sure to skip any products that might react to light. For example, because retinol and vitamin C serums are photosensitizing, they’re an absolute no-go before any LED treatment. Dr. Chacon adds that reflective products like heavy oils or sunscreens should also be avoided during use as they can scatter the light and disrupt the device’s effectiveness.

#4953. Arkansas Sues Kalshi, Joining a Growing List of States Targeting Prediction Markets

Arkansas Sues Kalshi, Joining a Growing List of States Targeting Prediction Markets

Arkansas has sued Kalshi, accusing the prediction market of offering an illegal, unregulated gambling platform to the state's residents, Attorney General Tim Griffin said on Wednesday.

The lawsuit, filed against KalshiEX LLC and Kalshi Inc., alleges multiple violations of the Arkansas Deceptive Trade Practices Act. The state is seeking restitution, penalties, taxes and an injunction.

Today I filed a lawsuit against @Kalshi for violating the Arkansas Deceptive Trade Practices Act by operating an unregulated gambling platform.

Kalshi has built a global business that it calls a prediction market, allowing consumers to wager money on the outcome of just about… pic.twitter.com/ePycNKXzy6

— Attorney General Tim Griffin (@AGTimGriffin) November 4, 2026

"It doesn't take a genius to understand that wagering money on whether the Razorbacks win or lose is gambling," Griffin said. "And gambling on an unregulated platform is squarely prohibited by Arkansas law."

Instead of obtaining licenses and paying the taxes required of gambling operators, Kalshi opened its platform to Arkansas residents as young as 18 and encouraged them to bet on sporting and entertainment events, Griffin alleged. "Arkansas law is concerned with substance, not semantics," he said.

Griffin pointed to Kalshi's own responsible-gambling tools, including self-exclusion, deposit limits and trading breaks, along with its partnership with the National Council on Problem Gambling, as evidence that the company knows its platform carries gambling-style risks. "Although Kalshi insists that its products are not gambling, its actions speak louder than its labels," he said.

Kalshi, which is regulated by the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, has argued in other cases that its event contracts fall under exclusive federal jurisdiction and are not subject to state gambling laws.

Arkansas is the latest of more than a dozen states to take on prediction markets. Among the most significant cases:

Other states have also moved against prediction markets. Arizona filed criminal charges against Kalshi in March, a Washington court temporarily blocked its sports contracts in July, and Massachusetts' case is now before the state's highest court after a lower court barred Kalshi's sports contracts in January.

The CFTC has sued at least 10 states to defend what it says is its exclusive authority over prediction markets, and President Donald Trump has backed the agency, calling several state officials who challenged the platforms "scum."

Federal appeals courts are split. The Third Circuit ruled in April that New Jersey cannot regulate Kalshi's sports contracts, while the Sixth and Ninth Circuits sided with Ohio, Tennessee and Nevada. New Jersey has asked the Supreme Court to settle the question, backed by 39 other states and the District of Columbia, as well as the NFL. Kalshi's response to the petition is due November 9, and the Supreme Court is not expected to decide whether to take up the case until December at the earliest, ESPN reported.

#4952. How Aussie Bitcoiners can avoid 2027’s shock CGT hike

From setting up an SMSF or a company, to just leaving the country entirely, here are some strategies Australian Bitcoiners can employ to deal with 2027’s massive CGT hike.

The Australian government’s decision to scrap the 50% capital gains discount for all investments in the May budget came as a big shock for crypto owners. The new scheme effectively doubles the taxable component of a gain, minus a small discount for inflation.

But there are legal ways to minimize the amount of CGT you’ll have to pay — if you start prepping now.

Some Aussie crypto holders are taking drastic measures — like Finder founder and crypto king Fred Schebesta who is relocating to Singapore which offers 0% CGT, stating the tax changes were a “killer blow.”But there are less dramatic methods to reduce your CGT bills including setting up a self managed super fund (SMSF), a company to trade crypto at the company tax rate, or to just cross your fingers and hope the opposition will reverse the changes after the next election.

Since 1999, Aussie asset owners have only paid tax on half of their capital gain. From July 1, 2027 they’ll pay their full income tax rate — which means 30% CGT if your income and gains are between A$45,000 and $135,000, 37% for the portion between $135,000 and $190,000 and 47% above that. (All figures in this story are in Aussie dollars)

Adding insult to injury, low-income earners who aren’t on welfare like students and retirees will pay a minimum 30% CGT rate no matter how little the gain.

The only discount now available accounts for the effect of inflation on your cost basis. So if you buy a house for $1 million in 2027 and sell it for $1.5 million after 10 years, with inflation at 3%, you won’t have to pay tax on the $344,000 or so attributed to inflation.

“The challenge for cryptocurrency is that the CGT changes, indexation really helps slower growing assets not fast ones,” explains Andrea Yuen, Swyftx CEO.

So if you bought Bitcoin for $10,000 and sell it ten years later for $100,000, the inflation discount is only around $3,439. Under the old system you would pay tax on $45,000 of gains. Under the new system you’ll pay tax on $86,561 of gains.

Stockspot analyst Chris Bryki compared how ten different asset classes would fare under the new rules. “Crypto sits at the bottom because almost its entire return comes from capital growth,” he wrote in The Australian newspaper.

Here are a few different strategies to help crypto holders deal with the changes, ranging from little to no effort, to restructuring your entire life.

Any gain you’ve made before July 1 2027 will be taxed under the existing 50% discount scheme. So your best bet is to hope a raging bull market pushes the value of your portfolio as high as possible before that date.You should faithfully record the value of every crypto asset you have that day, as any gain from that value will be taxed at the higher rate.

Ben Simpson, founder of crypto advisory service Collective Shift, says Bitcoin’s performance since mid-year in a challenging macro environment bodes well for further gains before July.

But Simpson (who points out he’s not a financial advisor) adds, “I probably wouldn’t be betting the farm on the fact that we’re gonna have a huge bull run”

Yuen notes that prices aren’t within your control.  “A strategy isn’t a strategy if you’re just sort of hoping,” she says.

Both the official opposition The Coalition, and the insurgent opposition One Nation, have promised to reverse the changes. Crypto voters who feel strongly enough about this single issue, could vote for a change in government.However, the ruling ALP holds 94 out of 150 seats in the House of Representatives and will be difficult to dislodge from office. If the government does change — and the legislation can pass the Senate in time — it’s technically possible the changes could be reversed before anyone has to file taxes from July 1 2028.

But it’s probably more likely that any change to the system would occur after that date. Adrian Forza, director of Crypto Tax Australia, says this would make calculating gains very difficult.

Australian retirement funds are called superannuation funds, and once you are over 60 you can access your investments tax-free — even as they keep growing.Unfortunately none of the retail super funds allow clients to invest in crypto, so if you want to protect your crypto gains using this method, you’d have to set up an SMSF. About a third of Simpson’s high net worth clients invest in crypto via SMSFs.  “Buying Bitcoin and accumulating it there is a super solid idea,” he says, although he cautions against adding many altcoins. “You’re risking your retirement, which is an ultra long-term hold, on short-term altcoins,” he says.

Australian super fund AMP has invested in Bitcoin — but doesn’t allow its clients to do so.

The big drawback with SMSFs — explains the 27-year-old founder — is that younger crypto investors can’t access the money for decades.

Realistically then, it’s a strategy more suited to wealthier investors aged over 50, who have a shorter period to wait.

Related: Older investors are risking everything for a crypto-funded retirement

The government has also made running an SMSF a pain in the backside, with lots of compliance paperwork and audits, as well as a bunch of unavoidable fees that make it prohibitive for smaller balances. Annual costs typically range between $2,000 and $4,000, although online providers like Stake can reduce total annual costs to around $1,330, as long as you only buy Bitcoin or Ether ETFs.

You also have to sell any crypto and pay tax on it before you can rebuy it with your SMSF, and there are annual contribution limits to factor in, although Forza says you should be able to get almost $400,000 in there with a bit of creativity.

Keep an eye out in December for a detailed report on how to set up a crypto SMSF.

A strategy some billionaires employ to avoid paying tax is by borrowing against their assets, as repaying the loan often works out cheaper than paying tax on a sale.

Bitcoiners can now access a range of lenders, including BlockEarner, Vield and Swyftx, that let you employ a similar strategy by borrowing against your BTC as collateral.

“We like to bring that strategy to the masses, not just to billionaires,” laughs Yuen.

But it can be risky if you don’t have a billionaire’s deep pockets. Borrowers face high interest rates, relatively short loan periods (typically 12 months), and risk losing their Bitcoin if the market moves against them. You can’t borrow against the full value either — Swyftx has a loan-to-value ratio around 40%.”If your collateral drops below a certain amount, there will be a margin call,” she says.  “I would say it’s a bet on a price going up. I think you have to have a view that crypto will go up — or at least not go down so that you get margin-called... Otherwise, a crypto-backed loan wouldn’t make sense for you.”

Simpson doesn’t recommend this strategy due to the risks, but says it can pay off for some people. For example if you borrow at 10% interest, and Bitcoin compounds by 33% a year (as it has done in the past) you’d end up much better off than paying CGT.

But he warns it’s a leveraged bet that is best suited to sophisticated investors.

Bitcoin is down 28% over the past year, so anyone borrowing a year ago would have faced margin calls and possible liquidation as well as interest costs.

Borrowing against Bitcoin for a house deposit has become a more popular strategy. (BlockEarner)

Forza cautions that borrowing crypto can also be a CGT event in some circumstances, such as borrowing in DeFi. If you deposit ETH to Aave, it gets automatically swapped to WETH which  is a taxable event, and when you withdraw the ETH it’s another taxable event.

Borrowing from centralized lenders like BlockEarner or Swyftx is currently a grey area, and the ATO has not clarified if it’s a taxable event.”If you’re talking about [borrowing] ten grand fine. But if you’ve got three to five Bitcoin, I wouldn’t risk it because if it goes against you then you’re going to be paying tax.””Vield is the only one in Australia that’s got a private ruling that says their product is not a taxable event. No one else has got one.”Swftxy confirmed it has not got a private ruling and said clients should seek tax advice, while BlockEarner did not respond to a request for comment.

In part two of our Aussie CGT special, coming later this week, we’ll bring you the final four strategies to minimize your crypto tax bills, including setting up a company or leaving the country entirely.

#4951. Canadian Man Extradited to US Over Plot to Hijack University Supercomputers for Crypto

Canadian Man Extradited to US Over Plot to Hijack University Supercomputers for Crypto

A Canadian man has been extradited to the United States to face charges that he helped hack into university computers to steal login credentials and secretly mine cryptocurrency, the Justice Department said on Tuesday.

A federal grand jury in the Western District of New York indicted Ryan James Roach, 35, of Saskatchewan, and Mathiew James Stubbings, 41, of Ontario, on charges of conspiracy to obtain information from and damage a protected computer, obtaining information from a protected computer, and intentionally damaging a protected computer. The charges carry a maximum sentence of 10 years in prison and a $250,000 fine.

Canadian man extradited to the U.S. for his role in computer hacking scheme targeting universities... @FBIBuffalo @USDOJ_Intl https://t.co/vY8IacXdZZ pic.twitter.com/NsSZ5BIkIf

Between June 2016 and December 2019, the pair targeted U.S. educational institutions with supercomputers, researching security weaknesses in their networks before breaking in, according to the indictment. Once inside, they allegedly deployed malware, stole login credentials and forced the victims' computers to mine cryptocurrency for their own benefit.

Victims included institutions in New York, Tennessee, North Carolina and Massachusetts, among them a university in the Western District of New York, prosecutors said. The pair allegedly used virtual private servers in several countries, registered under false names, to send malware and mining software to victim computers and store stolen credentials, and routed attacks through computers compromised at other victims to hide their identities.

Roach was extradited from Canada on October 28 and arraigned in Rochester, New York, where he was held pending a detention hearing. Extradition proceedings against Stubbings are continuing in Canada.

The scheme is a form of cryptojacking, in which attackers use other people's computers to mine crypto while the victims bear the electricity costs and slower performance. The Justice Department did not say which cryptocurrency the pair allegedly mined.

But hackers looking to profit from stolen computing power may be turning elsewhere. In a report published Monday, Google's Threat Intelligence Group said it expects attackers to "increasingly prioritize LLMJacking over traditional resource theft like cryptojacking," hijacking companies' access to AI models and passing on the bills.

Unlike the gradual drain of cryptojacking, LLMjacking can run up huge cloud charges within hours, Google said. In incidents investigated by its Mandiant unit this year, fraudulent charges topped $100,000 in a single day, and some organizations racked up $1 million before access was cut off. Attackers are reselling stolen access through underground services that Google calls "LLM Mixers."

#4950. Binance Cuts Off SignalPlus API Keys Over 'Potential Security Issue' at Trading Platform

Binance Cuts Off SignalPlus API Keys Over 'Potential Security Issue' at Trading Platform

Binance has removed all API keys linked to SignalPlus, a third-party crypto trading platform, after SignalPlus reported a "potential security issue," the exchange said on Thursday.

"Binance's own systems are unaffected. User funds remain safe," the exchange said, describing the move as an "immediate precautionary measure."

Following a potential security issue reported by third-party platform SignalPlus, we have proactively removed all API keys linked to SignalPlus as an immediate precautionary measure.

Binance's own systems are unaffected. User funds remain safe.

If you used SignalPlus, please… pic.twitter.com/0HYYkTAW2p

API keys let third-party apps and trading tools connect to a user's exchange account and place trades on their behalf. Removing them cuts off that connection, meaning any automated strategies running on Binance through SignalPlus will stop until users set up new keys.

Binance urged SignalPlus users to log into their accounts directly to review their positions and open orders. It recommended that they wait until SignalPlus confirms the issue is fully resolved before reconnecting, and that they always enable IP whitelisting, which restricts an API key to approved internet addresses.

The exchange also called on all third-party platforms integrated with Binance to review their security controls, watch for unusual activity and contact it immediately if they spot any concerns.

SignalPlus, a Hong Kong-based platform that provides trading tools for crypto options and derivatives, connects to several exchanges, including Binance.

The incident comes a week after Binance was hit by the largest hack in crypto history, in which attackers drained about $6.4 billion from its hot wallets on October 29. The FBI has attributed the theft to North Korean hackers it calls TraderTraitor, a claim Pyongyang has denied.

Binance has said the attack began when a fake recruiter persuaded one of its engineers to download a malware-laced coding assignment, and that the attackers then used stolen credentials to reach an internal wallet signing service.

Binance said on Wednesday that it has replenished about 98% of the stolen assets from its own funds, and is still buying the rest, mostly less liquid tokens, through market and over-the-counter channels. It has also taken a new proof-of-reserves snapshot covering 44 tokens, up from 10, which users will be able to verify once its zero-knowledge proof is generated in about 50 to 60 hours.

The exchange is still restoring services. On Thursday, it began reopening withdrawals for more assets in phases, prioritizing tokens based on user demand over the past week. Deposits remain open.

#4949. Crypto PACs eye 2028 ‘new map’ as Democrats take Congress

Crypto super PACs spent more than $176 million on the midterms, but the Democratic sweep raises the prospect of tougher industry scrutiny.

Two years after crypto money helped drive Sherrod Brown out of the Senate, roughly $64 million couldn’t keep him from returning.

Brown’s victory over Republican Jon Husted in Ohio delivered the industry its biggest electoral loss of the 2026 midterms as Democrats won control of both chambers of Congress. The sweep also puts Sen. Elizabeth Warren, one of crypto’s fiercest critics, in line to chair the Senate Banking Committee.

Crypto super PACs spent about $176 million across 100 seats during the election cycle, including primaries, according to Reuters, up from more than $130 million in 2024. Fairshake and its affiliated PACs accounted for $154.5 million across 89 races.

“The next Congress will bring a fundamentally different environment for crypto in Washington, and the industry should expect significantly more scrutiny,” Kyle Bligen, executive director at the Decentralization Research Center, told Cointelegraph.

Fairshake, meanwhile, is left with more than $67 million heading to the 2028 elections. “Newly elected and current members will have a choice: protect consumers and American jobs or fight progress,” spokesperson Geoff Vetter told Cointelegraph in an email. “We will be watching and ‘28 is a new map.”

Ohio was the industry’s biggest bet as crypto PACs put more than $64 million into keeping Senator Jon Husted in office. His opponent, former Sen. Sherrod Brown, won the special election to complete former Sen. JD Vance’s term, which ends in January 2029. He could seek a senior role on the Senate Agriculture Committee, which oversees the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC).

Brown’s comeback came two years after crypto’s biggest win in 2024, when Fairshake spent $40 million to help Bernie Moreno unseat him in Ohio’s other Senate seat. Some industry leaders treated that result as a warning to Congress. Gemini exchange co-founder Cameron Winklevoss declared on X: “This is what happens when you mess with the crypto army”.

This year, crypto spending was part of a much larger flood of outside money. Independent expenditures in the race topped $350 million, while Brown turned the crypto spending into a campaign theme, writing “I’ll be damned if crypto billionaires decide this race,” in a campaign message.

The loss in Ohio may weaken the perception that defying crypto PACs carries an electoral cost in Washington. Before the vote, a Republican crypto lobbyist told Politico that a Brown win would “neuter Fairshake’s perceived Sword of Damocles.”

Nebraska, meanwhile, offered crypto PACs a significant win, with Sen. Pete Ricketts retaining his seat after Fairshake affiliate Defend American Jobs spent $13.5 million opposing independent challenger Dan Osborn.

“As the biggest spender in the Nebraska Senate race, we’re proud to have helped Sen. Ricketts weather the waves of a hard-fought cycle,” wrote Vetter. “Fairshake played a meaningful role in winning competitive races across the country in 2026 and we will do it again in 2028.” The PAC’s other Senate wins came in Republican strongholds, including Barry Moore in Alabama and Andy Barr in Kentucky.

Other spending failed to deliver the intended results, both in primaries and in November. The Fairshake network’s roughly $10 million spent opposing Juliana Stratton in the Illinois Democratic primary failed to block her nomination. She later won the Senate seat. In Massachusetts, John Deaton lost despite roughly $700,000 in spending by crypto-backed Commonwealth Unity Fund to help elect him. Ken Paxton, who received crypto PAC backing in his successful Texas Republican runoff against John Cornyn, went on to lose to James Talarico.

The House brought another setback when digital assets subcommittee chair Bryan Steil lost to Democrat Mitchell Berman despite $1.9 million in crypto PAC funding.

The shift in congressional control could slow crypto legislation, but BitGo CEO and co-founder Mike Belshe said the election results would not alter the industry’s trajectory. “Crypto has continued to move forward under different administrations and different political majorities. The GENIUS Act is now law, stablecoin rules are moving ahead, and regulators are continuing to work on tokenization and digital asset markets.”Democratic victories could also bring crypto supporters into the Senate. Advocacy group Stand With Crypto counted 19 winning Senate candidates it classified as pro-crypto and eight as anti-crypto, with 33 of the 35 Senate races called as of 9:45 p.m. UTC on Nov. 4.

Despite the crypto PAC spending against him in Texas, Talarico voted for the Texas Bitcoin reserve as a state lawmaker and is rated by the group as strongly supportive of crypto.

For industry advocates, the next challenge is building relationships with incoming lawmakers. Jessica Martinez, US policy director at Fireblocks, told Cointelegraph that many newly elected members had limited records on crypto, making “educating members early” key to shaping the Democratic policy agenda.

#4948. Stablecoins Go Mainstream as Samsung Wallet Adds USDC on Solana

Stablecoins Go Mainstream as Samsung Wallet Adds USDC on Solana

Samsung is putting digital dollars in the same app where its users keep their credit cards and boarding passes.

Stablecoin accounts went live in Samsung Wallet on Thursday for eligible U.S. Galaxy users, letting them buy, hold, send, and receive Circle’s USDC on the Solana blockchain without downloading a separate crypto app or managing private keys. The feature reaches about 82 million compatible Galaxy devices in the U.S., according to the announcement.

“For a decade, stablecoins have proved the technology works,” said Lily Liu, president of the Solana Foundation. “What they lacked was a way to reach the mainstream.”

Samsung first announced the feature on Oct. 7, when the electronics giant tapped Solana for USDC transfers. Samsung Wallet first teased plans to support the stablecoin, which is pegged to the U.S. dollar, over the summer.

The product is built with Bastion, a licensed stablecoin custodian and infrastructure provider, and Coinbase, which safeguards the USDC as Bastion’s sub-custodian through its Coinbase Prime Vault. Samsung’s disclosures stress that the account is a crypto wallet, not a bank account.

Users can send USDC to other Samsung Wallet users in the U.S. and to compatible outside crypto wallets with no Samsung transfer fee, though other providers may charge one. They can also send money to qualifying bank accounts in more than 60 countries, where recipients may receive local currency, with fees that vary by destination.

Topping up with a credit card, including through Samsung Pay, carries a 4% fee, and withdrawals to a bank account or debit card also come with a fee. New users who make a first top-up of $50 or more can receive $25 in USDC. The service requires Android 13 or later and is open to U.S. residents 18 and older, except those in New York.

"Stablecoins have the potential to make moving money around the world faster and easier, and we want Galaxy users to be able to take advantage of that without having to navigate the complexity of traditional crypto tools,” said Woncheol Chai, who leads the digital wallet team in Samsung’s mobile business. “Solana helps us bring that experience to Samsung's scale."

Samsung said it expects to explore online and in-store stablecoin payments and to expand to additional markets, subject to local regulation.

#4947. BitMart founder says security team ‘severely understaffed’ after new hack

The crypto exchange’s founder warned of security staffing shortages amid restructuring as analysts flagged suspicious transfers potentially linked to the Mercado Bitcoin hack.

Cryptocurrency exchange BitMart is operating with an understaffed security team following restructuring, its founder said Wednesday, after confirming a new hack that may be linked to the recent attack on Brazil’s exchange Mercado Bitcoin.

In a post on X, BitMart founder Sheldon Xia said threats against employees and alleged retaliation by former staff had complicated efforts to retain technical personnel.

“It took considerable effort and individual conversations to persuade some key technical staff to remain and maintain our core systems,” Xia wrote. “However, we continue to face numerous attacks. Our security team is currently severely understaffed, but we are still doing everything we can to safeguard data and assets.”

Xia described the amount involved in Monday’s hack as small but did not disclose a figure. Blockchain analytics firm Bitquery identified approximately $391,000 in suspicious transfers from wallets mostly associated with BitMart across three blockchains, according to a Wednesday investigation.

Bitquery traced the funds to a wallet also implicated in the more than $46 million Mercado Bitcoin hack on Monday, Nov. 2, supporting Xia’s preliminary suspicion of a possible connection between the incidents.

Related: North Korea denies role in $6.3B Binance hack, points finger at US

The latest breach comes amid months of uncertainty over BitMart’s future. The exchange announced in July that it would end trading on Aug. 26 and cease operations in January 2027. Following the announcement, users reported delayed withdrawals and frozen accounts, raising concerns about access to their funds.

In August, BitMart’s Chinese-language X account alleged that employees had gone unpaid and demanded answers about customer funds. The founder dismissed the allegations as fabricated, though it remained unclear who controlled the account at the time.

In a restructuring proposal published Monday, the exchange outlined three potential recovery options for users, including liquid asset distributions and recovery-linked tokens, with plans to seek court approval in December or January.

Cointelegraph contacted BitMart for details about the restructuring, security staffing and latest breach but had not received a response by publication.

Separately, a crypto crime-fighting alliance formed by Tron, Tether and TRM Labs has dissolved amid a wave of major industry hacks, Bloomberg reported Wednesday.

The T3 Financial Crime Unit, launched in September 2024 and later joined by Binance, struggled with slow responses and poor coordination, Bloomberg reported, citing people familiar with the matter. One source said the group disbanded in late August.

In May, T3 said it had helped freeze more than $450 million in assets linked to suspected criminal activity since its launch in 2024.

The group’s dissolution comes amid a wave of major crypto hacks, including last week’s $6.3 billion Binance breach, the largest cryptocurrency theft on record.

#4946. Morning Minute: Democrat Sweep Pumps Crypto as Clarity Act Resurfaces

Morning Minute: Democrat Sweep Pumps Crypto as Clarity Act Resurfaces

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

🗳️ Democrat Sweep Pumps Crypto as Market Structure Bill Resurfaces

Bitcoin spent Tuesday night under $76,000, a seven-week low, with traders bracing for whatever a Democratic Congress meant for crypto. By Wednesday afternoon it was back above $80,800, up 4% on the day.

The easy story is that uncertainty lifted and crypto exhaled. And that could be part of it. But oil helped. WTI fell 9% to $88 a barrel, down almost 17% on the week, as Iran’s parliament speaker said voters had “said NO TO WAR.” The DOW jumped 3% on the day and Nasdaq +1.6% in what was a huge green day across markets.

James Butterfill of CoinShares put it plainly: “For Bitcoin, real yields, dollar liquidity and ETP flows still carry more weight than the elections.”

But the policy news that landed Wednesday afternoon matters more than the candles.

Eleanor Terrett reported that House Democrats are discussing expanding Maxine Waters’ Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights, originally an amendment to the CLARITY Act in 2025, into a full market structure bill next Congress. The draft would fold in Democratic CLARITY provisions that the House rejected last year, plus provisions Senate Democrats and Republicans had already agreed on before the September cloture vote that killed the bill. Three industry sources described the talks to her.

That’s a genuine surprise. Twenty-four hours ago the consensus was that market structure legislation was dead for two years. Waters is the likely incoming chair of House Financial Services, and she is apparently planning to write a bill rather than hold hearings. But the bill will have some changes.

The new proposals under discussion would give the SEC a larger role supervising crypto markets and leave the CFTC with a smaller one. The draft is expected to carry ethics provisions restricting the crypto activities of Trump and other federal officials. Those provisions are what Democrats wanted all along, and what many thought would kill any chances the bill has of getting signed.

So one of the first courses of action from this Democrat-led Congress is to enact some reasonable changes to the Clarity Act to try to get it passed in 2027. That feels like a very bullish first step.

#4944. Sam Altman Says It's 'Fucking Annoying' His RAM Comments Were Taken Out of Context

Sam Altman Says It's 'Fucking Annoying' His RAM Comments Were Taken Out of Context

OpenAI CEO Sam Altman has hit back at criticism of his comments about computer memory, saying they were taken out of context and that he has long argued for the world to make more of it.

"I actually do want the world to have tons of memory and people to put as much as they want in their computers, and it is fucking annoying that this was taken out of context," Altman tweeted early Wednesday. "I have been arguing for the world to ramp up memory production for a long time."

but only because...i actually do want the world to have tons of memory and people to put as much as they want in their computers, and it is fucking annoying that this was taken out of context.

i have been arguing for the world to ramp up memory production for a long time.

The post came as Altman leaned into the backlash. Replying to users joking about his remarks, he said he "lol'd" at one meme and, when another asked him to rename his X account "Ram Altman," replied "ok yeah," briefly changing his display name before explaining he had only done so because he wanted people to have plenty of memory.

Altman's original comments came in an interview with YouTube channel Cheeky Pint, where he discussed shortages of memory and other components caused by the AI boom. "From like a global allocation perspective, actually, it's quite terrible to have everybody have a bunch of the world's limited supply of DRAM sitting in their own computer and being used 5% of the time," he said. "You want maximum utilization in a cloud if you're trying to deal with this very supply-constrained environment."

In the same interview, Altman also blamed memory makers for being slow to expand production, and said he would be "very sad" if prices for chips and other components did not fall.

The remarks drew widespread criticism, including from Elon Musk, who mocked Altman on Sunday with a meme of the Inglourious Basterds character Hans Landa asking, "You are withholding DRAM from the cloud, are you not?" "The people deserve to have their RAM too!" Musk wrote.

The spat comes amid a global memory shortage driven by demand from AI data centers. SK hynix, one of the world's largest memory makers, warned last week that demand for memory would outstrip supply through 2030.

Contract prices for 16GB DDR5 memory chips rose from $8.70 in October 2025 to $48 in September 2026, an increase of more than 550%, according to Tom's Hardware. On an earnings call this week, Chen Ching-wen, CEO of memory maker Team Group, said there was "no downward price trend whatsoever," and warned that some buyers could face a situation where "even money can't buy goods."

The crunch has already pushed up prices for consumer devices. In April, Meta raised the price of its Quest 3 and Quest 3S headsets by $50 to $100, weeks after Sony increased PlayStation 5 prices.

#4943. India warns AI-powered hackers targeting crypto exchanges at ‘machine speed’

India’s cybersecurity agency says a threat actor is escalating an AI-assisted hacking campaign targeting crypto exchanges and other financial firms.

India’s national cybersecurity agency warned that an AI-assisted threat actor has escalated its attacks on cryptocurrency exchanges and other financial businesses, stealing wallet keys and siphoning funds.

The Indian Computer Emergency Response Team (CERT-In) on Wednesday designated the threat actor as “CRAFTY FALCON,” in an update to a campaign it first warned about in October. The latest advisory has now explicitly identified crypto exchanges among affected businesses and detailed the use of AI attack tools.

“The threat is active and ongoing. CSIRT-Fin continues to receive reports of successful attacks, including from NBFCs and fintech entities that had already received the earlier restricted advisories and had undertaken remediation,” said CERT-In.

The warning adds to evidence of hackers using AI tools against financial institutions. In October, cybersecurity firm CrowdStrike reported that an unidentified attacker used ARTEX, an AI penetration-testing tool also named in CERT-In’s advisory, alongside Claude Code and other AI models to breach South Korean financial firms and steal data.

CERT-In said the AI-assisted financial threat actor “surveys its targets with machine intelligence, operates at machine speed and strikes by invoking the victim’s own payment and disbursement APIs.”

The campaign has targeted nonbank lenders, digital lending platforms, fintech companies, payment aggregators and service providers, as well as crypto exchanges.

According to CERT-In, attackers exploited vulnerable applications and exposed testing environments, using shared credentials and access tokens to move into production systems. They also targeted cloud infrastructure to deploy malicious files.

AI tools helped attackers rapidly generate and refine attack scripts. The attackers also reverse-engineered application code to develop tailored exploits, while some attacks were timed for weekends and holidays to delay detection.

Related: Binance traces $6.3B hack to fake job interviews, compromised AI tool

In some incidents, attackers used small test transactions to validate unauthorized transfers before moving larger sums. CERT-In also reported stolen cryptocurrency wallet keys and funds drained from wallets.

The agency did not name any affected crypto exchanges or quantify losses.

Cointelegraph reached out to CERT-In, CoinDCX, CoinSwitch, WazirX, ZebPay and Mudrex for comment.

CERT-In has advised entities to “treat this campaign as active and escalating,” urging at-risk companies to heighten security monitoring around the clock, with increased staffing and lowered alert thresholds during holidays, weekends and other low-coverage windows.

Solana Foundation chief information security officer Michael Coates previously told Cointelegraph that legitimate security teams need access to the strongest AI tools.

“We need to make sure that the best models we can get are in the hands of defenders because attackers will have something capable enough,” he said.

TRM Labs head of policy Ari Redbord similarly told Cointelegraph in July that “attack techniques are moving faster than a single audit from launch day can account for,” urging continuous security reviews.

#4942. Navigating the Holidays When You're Estranged From Family

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 3, 2026 | Reviewed by Gary Drevitch

Knowing you had to cut ties with your parent to protect yourself doesn't make the holidays any easier. It's normal to miss having a family to celebrate with, feel guilty about not being together, or dread questions about why you're not seeing your parents. Even when you know you've made the right decision, the holidays can bring up feelings you thought you'd worked through.

Here's how to navigate the season in a way that protects your well-being and helps you regain some joy.

For many adult children of dysfunctional parents, holidays can be painful reminders of both your current reality and what you've lost. You may find yourself grieving the parent you're estranged from, but also the parent you always wished you'd had. It's completely natural to feel confused or upset; part of you may still long for a nurturing, loving connection, while another part knows that staying in a toxic relationship would only cause more pain.

Holidays can amplify these feelings because they're built around an idealized version of family that's never been your reality. You may feel pressure to attend gatherings that feel unsafe, or you may be flooded with painful memories when everyone around you seems to be celebrating. The goal is to learn to tolerate such feelings, knowing that they will lessen, and to treat yourself with compassion.

The first decision is whether to attend family events at all. This is always a choice, even when it doesn't feel like one. Choosing not to attend a gathering when your estranged parent will be there isn’t necessarily a sign of avoidance but of maintaining a boundary. If you do choose to attend, it should be because it genuinely serves you—and not because of guilt, obligation, or pressure from others.

Take time to weigh your options. Ask yourself: What's the emotional cost of attending? What do you hope to gain—and is it realistic? And will being there require you to compromise your boundaries or safety? (For people with abusive or toxic parents, skipping a holiday gathering may be the healthiest decision.)

Once you've made your decision, preparation can help you avoid reactive choices in an already emotional situation. Anxiety can build for days or weeks before a difficult holiday, so don't wait until the day arrives to think through your approach.

If you're not attending: Resist the pull to leave the day unstructured. A completely open day can intensify rumination and sadness. Plan something intentional that gives you something grounded to move toward.

If you're attending: Go in with a clear plan so you feel more grounded and in control.

Estrangement invites you to rethink what holidays can look like. You don't have to organize your life around traditions that cause pain; giving yourself permission to step away is progress in itself.

Think creatively about how you want to spend the holiday. Start new traditions, volunteer, or do something you genuinely enjoy but rarely make time for—a hike, a movie, or a good book. It's also completely fine to use the time to rest, reflect, practice self-care, do chores, or pursue hobbies. There are no rules: How you spend the day is completely up to you, whether that means making new meaning out of the holiday or simply getting through it in whatever way feels manageable.

Holidays can be emotionally draining, regardless of what you decide to do. In my book, Cutting Ties with Your Parents, I share these strategies for getting through the season:

Journaling is one of the most effective tools for working through complicated emotions; it slows your thinking and brings clarity. Try reflecting on questions like, What am I grieving today? What do I wish I'd received from my parents that I didn't? and What do I need right now, and how can I give it to myself?

If anxiety or distressing memories surface, try a grounding exercise, deep breathing, or a guided meditation to help interrupt the stress response.

Estrangement can’t erase the hurt you've experienced, but it can create space for healing. With each holiday you navigate, you may build more clarity about who you are, what you need, and what matters most to you. With practice, you’ll move from simply surviving the season to building traditions and relationships that feel safer, healthier, and more joyful.
